# SUMMARY.md

File-level index of the codebase. **"Where do I look to change X?"** — answered here. Update when files are added, removed, or change responsibility.

Conventions: each entry is `path — purpose. Touchpoints.` Touchpoints list adjacent files you usually edit together.

---

## Backend — `lexy-app/backend/`

### Entry + infra
| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `main.py` | FastAPI app. Lifespan: pool init, phrase + grammar seeds, resume pending content requests. Mounts all routers. CORS env-driven via `CORS_ORIGINS` (`_parse_cors_origins`). |
| `database.py` | asyncpg pool create/close/get. Single global pool. TLS via `DB_SSL_MODE` (S4: `disable`/`require`/`verify-ca`/`verify-full`): `_resolve_ssl` for the asyncpg pool, `resolve_sslmode` (libpq string, omit-on-unset) reused by Alembic + the scraper. |
| `core/deps.py` | `get_current_user` JWT verification dependency. |
| `core/security.py` | password hashing (bcrypt), `encode_token` / `decode_token`. |
| `core/security_headers.py` | S5 middleware adding a conservative security-header baseline to **every** response, error responses and the SPA served from `frontend/dist` included. Registered in `main.py` as `SecurityHeadersMiddleware`. |
| `models/schemas.py` | All Pydantic request/response models. ~600 lines, one file. |
| `alembic.ini`, `migrations/env.py` | Alembic config. `env.py` builds the URL from `DB_*` env + appends `?sslmode=` via `database.resolve_sslmode` (S4). |
| `migrations/versions/0XX_*.py` | 37 migrations (head `037`), append-only. Schema lives here. |

### Routers (HTTP surface) — `lexy-app/backend/routers/`
| Path | Endpoints | Calls |
|---|---|---|
| `auth.py` | `/auth/register`, `/auth/login` | `auth_service` |
| `words.py` | `/words/by-text`, `/words/knowledge`, `/words/{type}/{id}/status`, `/words/word/{id}/transcript-click` | `word_service`, `progression_service`, `usage_events_service` |
| `srs.py` | `/srs/due`, `/srs/review/{card_id}` | `review_service` |
| `chat.py` | `/chat/sessions`, `/chat/sessions/{id}/messages`, `/chat/guided-sessions`, `/chat/guided-sessions/{id}/messages`, `/complete` | `chat_service`, `guided_chat_service`, `llm_service`, `progression_service` |
| `books.py` | `/books/upload`, `/books`, `/books/{id}`, `/books/{id}/pages`, `/books/{id}/pages/{n}`, `/books/{id}/pages/{n}/image`, `/blocks/{id}` PATCH, `/blocks/{id}/llm-repair`, `/pages/{n}/batch-llm-repair` | `book_service`, `book_llm_service` |
| `reading.py` | `/books/{id}/pages/{n}/word-statuses`, `/books/{id}/selections`, `/books/{id}/pages/{n}/selections`, `/reading/selections/due`, `/reading/selections/{id}` PATCH/DELETE, `/reading/selections/{id}/review`, `/reading/translate`, `/reading/explain` | `reading_service`, `reading_llm_service`, `progression_service`, `book_service` |
| `phrases.py` | `/phrases/match`, `/phrases/seed` | `matcher_service`, `phrase_service` |
| `matcher.py` | `/sentences/match` (shared with phrases) | `matcher_service` |
| `insights.py` | `/insights/cards`, `/insights/prep`, `/insights/prep/generate-examples` | `insights_service`, `llm_service` |
| `analytics.py` | `/analytics/unknown-frequent`, `/learning-frequent`, `/recently-failed`, `/most-interacted` | `usage_events_service` |
| `recommendations.py` | `/recommendations/sentences`, `/videos`, `/items` | `recommendation_service` |
| `playlists.py` | `/playlists/generate` | `playlist_service` |
| `settings.py` | `/settings/preferences` GET/PUT | `settings_service` |
| `reminders.py` | `/reminders/summary` | `reminder_service` |
| `search.py` | `/search`, `/suggest`, `/word-forms`, `/languages`, `/categories`, `/video-sentences` (legacy public, no auth) | `search_service` |
| `videos.py` | `/videos/{id}/reading-stats` | `reading_stats_service` |
| `content_requests.py` | `/content-requests` POST/GET. Keeps the S7 `content_id` validation (normalize to a canonical YouTube id at the API boundary) and the subprocess spawn of `subtitle-scraper/pipeline.py --requests-only`. The SQL moved to `content_request_service` (arch-05); the router still decides *whether* to spawn, from the returned `status`. | `content_request_service` + subprocess |
| `notifications.py` | `/notifications/stream` (SSE). Owns the `_yield_unseen` generator and its per-row mark-after-yield ordering (disconnect leaves un-yielded rows unseen for re-delivery) — that sequencing is deliberately **not** in the service, since it's about when the consumer resumes, not about the SQL. LISTEN/NOTIFY refactor deferred (TODO #4b). | `notification_service` |
| `word_lists.py` | `/word-lists` POST/GET, `/word-lists/{id}` GET/DELETE, `/word-lists/{id}/export` (text/plain), `/word-lists/{id}/mark-unknown-learning`. `GET /word-lists/{id}` takes optional `limit` (1–1000) and `offset` (≥0), which window **`entries` only** — `total` and `counts` stay whole-list on every page, so the status badges and the mark-learning count keep describing the list rather than the window. Omitting both returns the pre-pagination response unchanged. All routes user-scoped, 404 (not 403) on someone else's id — ownership resolves before the params are read, so paging cannot widen access. | `word_list_service`, `progression_service` |
| `account.py` | `DELETE /api/v1/account` — permanent account + owned-data deletion. Requires a valid bearer token **and** the current password in the request body (S3 re-auth); wrong/missing password → 403, account intact. Fan-out is by FK declaration, not by hand. See `docs/PRIVACY.md`. | `auth_service`, `core.security` |
| `lemma_corrections.py` | `POST /lemma-corrections` (auth + throttle, flag → candidate) + `GET /admin/lemma-corrections` (`require_admin`, queue) + `POST /admin/lemma-corrections/{id}/{accept,reject}` (`require_admin`; accept transactionally upserts `lemma_override`) + `…/{id}/adjudicate` (`require_admin`, read-only dry-run LLM proposal via injected `get_lemma_adjudicator` → 503 if none) — #39 3A/3B/3C. | `lemma_correction_service` |

### Services (business logic) — `lexy-app/backend/services/`
| Path | Owns |
|---|---|
| `progression_service.py` | **Single source of truth** for knowledge-state changes. `_RULES` dict maps event → ProgressionDelta. `apply_progression` is transactional (line 178). `_update_srs` runs SM-2 and now skips active-card creation for grammar_rule (line 268). |
| `review_service.py` | Real SRS implementation. `get_due_cards` joins per-type display table. `submit_answer` maps to `progression_service`. |
| `word_seed_service.py` | German catalog backfill from `data/final_result.txt` column 0 (N5a step 1). Article-strips, keeps clean single tokens, inserts only surfaces absent under `text_norm.normalize_key` with `pos=''` (a real POS would fork rows — `pos` is in the unique key). Never updates existing rows, never touches `phrase_table`. Driven by `scripts/backfill_word_catalog.py`. `inserted` comes from `INSERT ... RETURNING`, so a pre-existing row is never counted as written. |
| `text_norm.py` | `normalize_key` (NFC + strip + `lower()`) and `index_by_key` — the Unicode comparison key for catalog lookups. Exists because the DB is C-collation, where Postgres's `lower()`/`ILIKE` fold ASCII only, so `lower(col) = ANY(python_lowered)` never matched an umlaut-initial word. Uses `lower()` **not** `casefold()`: casefold maps ß→ss and would merge `schließen`/`schliessen` into one ambiguous key. |
| `catalog_resolver.py` | **Shared** surface → catalog row resolution, used by `word_list_service` and `reading_service`. Folds case in Python via `text_norm.normalize_key` (SQL `lower()`/`ILIKE` are ASCII-only under this DB's C collation). Shape-based precedence: multi-token → phrase first, single-token → word first. Phrases match `canonical`, **never** `surface_form` (773 German rows differ). Never first-matches: several candidates → `ambiguous` with `item_id=None`. `resolve_surfaces` (batch) + `resolve_one` (single). |
| `gloss_seed_service.py` | Pre-seeds curated English glosses from `data/words_4000_old.txt` into `llm_cache` under the sentinel model `curated:words_4000_old` (TODO #43 step 2). Words only; skips multi-entry cells, multi-sense and >4-word translations. Makes **no LLM calls**. Driven by `scripts/seed_gloss_cache.py` (dry-run default, `--apply` to write, `RETURNING`-based inserted count). |
| `system_list_seed_service.py` | Seeds the two built-in system lists from `data/final_result.txt` (col 0 → *Top German Words*, col 1 → *German Verb & Phrase Patterns*) via `catalog_resolver`, the same rule user lists use. **Keeps ambiguous and unresolved surfaces** with `item_id=NULL` rather than dropping or first-matching them. Idempotent by constraint: `ON CONFLICT (name) WHERE is_system` for the list, `ON CONFLICT (list_id, lower(surface))` for items — append-only. No LLM calls. Driven by `scripts/seed_system_lists.py` (dry-run default, `--apply`, `RETURNING` counts). |
| `WordListsPage.tsx` | Vocabulary lists UI. Splits the index into a **Built-in lists** section (`is_system`) and *Your lists*: built-ins carry a “Built-in” badge, show no Delete control, and keep Open / export / mark-unknown-learning. `SYSTEM_DISPLAY_NAME` overrides a stored list name for display only — the backend name is the seeding idempotency key. Detail entries are **fetched** a page at a time — `ENTRY_CHUNK` (200) is sent as the backend's `limit`, and “Show more” requests `offset=<entries loaded>` and appends. The footer denominator is `detail.total` (whole-list) while the numerator is `entries.length` (loaded), which is what makes it read “Showing 200 of 5,035” instead of “200 of 200”. Counts, export and mark-learning read the whole-list `total`/`counts`, which every page carries. `showDetail()` is the single funnel for *replacing* the open list and resets loading/error state; `handleShowMore` is the one deliberate exception because it *extends* rather than replaces. Late responses are guarded both ways: a page for a list the user navigated away from is dropped, and a late failure cannot overwrite the new list's error state. |
| `word_list_service.py` | User vocabulary lists (upload/paste → resolve → export). Resolves against **both `word_table` and `phrase_table`** (the latter seeded from `data/final_result.txt`), so a pasted blueprint binds to the same phrase row chat/SRS use. Precedence is shape-based: multi-token → phrase first, single-token → word first; several matches within the preferred type mean `ambiguous` and the fallback is not consulted. Five entry states: known/learning/unknown/**unresolved** / **ambiguous** (never auto-bound). `mark_unknown_as_learning` goes through `progression_service` with the resolved `item_type`; it never writes `user_word_knowledge` or `srs_cards`. Since migration 037 reads are `(user_id = $2 OR is_system)` so **built-in system lists** (owner-less, `is_system=true`) are visible to everyone; writes stay user-only, `delete_list` refuses a system list via its unchanged `user_id` filter, and `mark_unknown_as_learning` suppresses its late-binding UPDATE on shared rows while still applying per-user progression. `mark_unknown_as_learning` is **capped at MAX_LIST_WORDS (500) per call** — each entry costs its own `apply_progression` transaction, so an uncapped call on a 5,035-item built-in list would be thousands of sequential round-trips and could add ~9,600 SRS cards in one click, with no undo. The response carries `remaining`/`capped`; because the call is idempotent, capping degrades into chunking (call again). `get_list` takes optional `limit`/`offset` (also exposed as query params on `GET /word-lists/{id}`) that page **`entries` only** — `total` and `counts` stay whole-list on every page. Omitting both reproduces the pre-pagination response exactly. It shrinks the response, not the query: `_load_entries` still resolves the whole list because counts need it. |
| `word_service.py` | `resolve_word_ids` (Unicode-correct case-insensitive match via `text_norm.normalize_key` — **not** `ILIKE`, which folds ASCII only under this DB's C collation), `lookup_word_by_text` (resolves then enriches; ambiguous on POS, never first-matched), `learn_word_anyway` (**resolves before inserting** so an existing `pos=''`/scraper row is reused instead of forked — `ON CONFLICT (word, language, pos)` cannot see a row under a different `pos`), `get_user_knowledge`. (`upsert_word_status` was deleted 2026-05-19 — `progression_service.apply_progression(..., status_override=...)` is now the single writer.) |
| `chat_service.py` | session/message CRUD + `match_learning_words` (free-chat matching against the user's vocab — words **and** phrases, via `matcher_service.match_sentence_with_ids` for the phrase half). `match_learning_words` folds case in **Python** via `text_norm.normalize_key`, inverting the join (fetch the user's tracked non-known words, filter in Python) — SQL `LOWER()` folds ASCII only under this DB's C collation, so umlaut words earned no free-chat credit. The phrase half matches on spaCy-returned `phrase_id`s and never folds. |
| `guided_chat_service.py` | `get_next_target` (priority: due active → learning without active → random; considers words AND phrases at every tier), `update_progress` (event mapping). |
| `llm_provider.py` | **The only place an LLM client is constructed.** Two backends behind `LLM_PROVIDER`: `AnthropicProvider` (default) and `OpenAICompatibleProvider` (POSTs `{LLM_BASE_URL}/chat/completions` via `httpx`, `response_format=json_schema`, one retry falling back to `json_object`). Any host — built for a GPU desktop over Tailscale, not localhost. `LLMProviderError` carries provider/model/base-url and never the API key (`_redact` scrubs URL userinfo too). `LLMProvider` Protocol — `structured(system, messages, schema, max_tokens) -> dict` + `model_id`. `AnthropicProvider` maps a JSON Schema (`title`/`description` + body) onto a forced single-tool call; `split_schema` strips those two keys so the wire bytes match the pre-seam tool dicts. `LLMProviderError` replaces the old bare `StopIteration`. **No image/multimodal support yet** — both backends pass `messages` through verbatim, so adding it means a neutral image content-block normalizer here (the two wire formats diverge). See `docs/INGESTION_PIPELINE.md` §8. |
| `llm_service.py` | All Claude Haiku calls. tool_use for structured outputs. Cached via `llm_cache_service`. Has `MOCK_LLM=true` mode. `translate_item_gloss` reads the **curated** cache key (model `curated:words_4000_old`) before the model-specific one, so seeded human glosses survive an `LLM_MODEL` switch while genuine LLM output stays model-scoped. It never *writes* curated rows. |
| `llm_cache_service.py` | SHA256(prompt_key+model+params) → `llm_cache` table. TTL or permanent. |
| `book_service.py` | PDF upload, docling+masking ingestion, page/block CRUD, sentence_count, user_text_override. |
| `book_llm_service.py` | Per-block LLM OCR repair. Used by `/blocks/{id}/llm-repair` and batch. |
| `reading_service.py` | Selection CRUD, word-status lookup for page highlighting, `find_catalog_item` (selection → word/phrase id), `record_review` (own SRS schedule), `get_due_selections`. `find_catalog_item` delegates to `catalog_resolver` — Unicode-correct, matches phrase `canonical` (was `surface_form`), and **fails safe on ambiguity** instead of silently first-matching. `get_word_statuses_for_page` folds in Python and keys results with plain `.lower()` to match the frontend's JS `toLowerCase()` lookup (deliberately not NFC — the frontend has no `.normalize()`). |
| `reading_llm_service.py` | `translate_sentence`, `explain_in_context`. Permanent cache. |
| `reading_stats_service.py` | Lemma coverage stats for books/videos. |
| `insights_service.py` | `get_insight_cards` (frequent_unknowns, recent_mistakes), `get_prep_data` (translation + grammar + examples + linked rules). |
| `recommendation_service.py` | Pure scoring functions (score_sentence, score_video, channel_category_multiplier) + DB orchestration. Mixed item types go through `enrich_by_type` (dispatches to per-type enrichers for word/phrase/grammar_rule). `enrich_items` remains the word-only backend. |
| `prioritization_service.py` | `get_prioritized_items` — combines is_due (×4), mistake_recency (×3 decay), freq_rank (×2 linear), is_learning (×1). |
| `usage_events_service.py` | `record_event` (analytics fire-and-forget) + `record_transcript_click_event` (atomic dedup-aware insert) + 4 aggregations. Insight filters include `'transcript'` context. |
| `matcher_service.py` | Async wrapper around `subtitle-scraper/phrase_finder.py`. Imports the scraper via `sys.path.insert` (post-#3, no more `os.chdir`). `match_sentence` runs sync spaCy in a thread pool. Parses per-language (#39 slice 2): German via phrase_finder's resident model, others via `nlp_service`'s cache (`_model_for`, lock-guarded); `match_sentence_with_ids` loads `lemma_override` and threads it into the extractor so chat canonicals are corrected. |
| `phrase_service.py` | `seed_from_blueprint_map`, `enrich_phrases`, phrase_type inference from blueprint. |
| `lemma_correction_service.py` | `create_candidate` (INSERT…ON CONFLICT…report_count+1) + `list_candidates`; `accept_candidate` (transactional: upsert `lemma_override`, flip `accepted`) + `reject_candidate`; `adjudicate_candidate_dry_run` (read-only, injected adjudicator → advisory proposal) + `build_adjudication_input`/`format_adjudication_prompt` + domain exceptions (#39 3A/3B/3C). Accept is the only path from a user signal to `lemma_override`. |
| `grammar_service.py` | `seed_rules` (curated DE list), `get_rules_for_phrase_type`, `get_rules_for_lemma`. |
| `playlist_service.py` | Video playlist generation from target words. Two optimizers behind one signature: `greedy_cover` (default, no deps) and `ilp_cover` (opt-in `algorithm="ilp"`, PuLP/CBC, optimal under `max_videos`). ILP solves **maximum coverage under the cap**, not minimum set cover, so it degrades to a partial playlist instead of going infeasible. `pulp` imports lazily — missing solver → 503, greedy unaffected. No frontend selector yet: API-only. |
| `nlp_service.py` | spaCy wrapper utilities. |
| `auth_service.py` | register, login. |
| `settings_service.py` | get/update preferences from `users.settings` JSONB. Default values centralised here. |
| `reminder_service.py` | Learning reminder summary. |
| `content_request_service.py` | Every statement against `content_request` (arch-05 extraction from the router). `create_or_reset` — INSERT … ON CONFLICT `(user_id, request_type, content_id)`; resets **only** a `failed` row back to `pending` (clearing `error`), leaves `pending`/`done` untouched, returns the whole row (`RETURNING *`). `list_for_user` (newest first) and `count_pending` (global, feeds the startup resume in `main.py`'s lifespan). Deliberately does **not** spawn the scraper: it returns the row, the router reads `status` and decides. `content_id` is expected pre-validated by the router's S7 normalizer — the API is the table's only writer. |
| `notification_service.py` | Read side of the `notification` table: `fetch_unseen` (oldest first, returns asyncpg `Record`s so `payload` reaches the caller as-is) and `mark_seen` (single row). Delivery *ordering* is not here — `routers/notifications.py` interleaves these two around its yields to get re-delivery on disconnect. Write side lives in `subtitle-scraper/pipeline.py` (`_notify_user`), a separate process. |
| `rate_limiter.py` | Per-user sliding-window LLM throttle (#12): 30 req/min + 400 req/hour, wired via `core/deps.rate_limit_llm` into the 11 LLM-backed routes. Also holds the login / client-error / lemma-flag windows. **In-process** — a multi-worker deploy needs a Redis backend. |
| `srs_backfill_service.py` | One-shot repair (W6 / Holes 11+16): create the active SRS cards that pre-#0b `status_marked_learning` never made for existing `learning` items. |
| `srs_cleanup_service.py` | One-shot repair (Hole 10): delete `srs_cards` rows orphaned by a removed `user_word_knowledge` row. |
| `search_service.py` | Full-text video/subtitle search. Corpus `search` resolves the query to `word_id`s / `blueprint_id`s in Python via `text_norm.normalize_key`, then matches `= ANY(...)` — SQL `ILIKE`/`~*` fold ASCII only under this DB's C collation. `_resolve_blueprint_ids` also `re.escape`s the term (the old word-boundary predicate interpolated raw user input into a regex — SECURITY.md S19). No debug `print()` in the request path (seven were removed 2026-07-28). **Autocomplete is fixed too** (migration 036): `_suggest_words` matches a `normalize_key`-folded prefix against the generated `word_table.word_norm` column via `ix_word_table_lang_word_norm` (EXPLAIN-verified index scan), and `_suggest_phrases` matches `phrase_blueprint.lookup_key_norm` with the word-boundary check done in Python — Postgres's `\m`/`\M` are ctype-dependent and never match a non-ASCII token under the C locale. Both escape user input (`_escape_like_prefix`, `_escape_regex`). |

### Tests — `lexy-app/backend/tests/`
See `docs/TESTS.md` for full list, coverage status, and known failures.

## Frontend — `lexy-app/frontend/`

### Top level
| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/App.tsx` | Router + Layout. Outlet context provides token/prefs/actions to every page. |
| `src/main.tsx` | Vite entry. |
| `src/auth.ts` | `localStorage` token/email helpers. |
| `vite.config.ts` | Dev proxy `/api → :8000`. Vitest + jsdom config. |
| `package.json` | React 19, RR 7, Vite 8, TS 5.9, Vitest. |

### Pages (routed) — `src/App.tsx` declares
| Route | Component | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | `HomePage` | Search + PlayerView + Free/Guided chat |
| `/for-you` | `ForYouPage` → `RecommendationsPanel` | Items, videos, sentences + InsightsSection |
| `/playlist` | `PlaylistPage` | Build playlists from target words |
| `/books` | `BooksPage` → `BookLibraryPage` / `BookReaderPage` | Upload, list, read PDFs |
| `/review` | `ReviewPage` → `SRSReviewPage` | SRS due cards. Passive = English-gloss prompt + reveal + self-grade; active = typed German + LLM evaluation via `/srs/review/{id}/produce`. |
| `/add-content` | `AddContentPage` → `ContentRequestPage` | Channel/video requests |
| `/settings` | `SettingsPage` → `SettingsPanel` | Prefs, colours, reps, channels, dark mode |

### Components — `src/components/`
Grouped by feature:

**Search / Player flow**
- `SearchBar.tsx` — multi-term search input
- `PlayerView.tsx` — main result player with tabs
- `YoutubeEmbed.tsx` — iframe with seekTo/pause/play
- `PlayerControls.tsx` — prev/next/replay
- `SubtitleDisplay.tsx` — clickable highlighted text
- `TranscriptPanel.tsx` — sentence list with statuses (has its own test)

**Word interaction**
- `WordStatusPicker.tsx` — modal lookup + status switcher. Offers "Learn this anyway" when the surface isn't in the catalog (W2 / Hole 1); shows a candidate chooser when `/by-text` returns `status='ambiguous'` (W3 / Hole 2).
- `TurnFeedbackChip.tsx` — eval chip in chat

**Chat**
- `ChatWindow.tsx`, `MessageInput.tsx` — generic message UI
- `FreeChatPage.tsx`, `GuidedChatPage.tsx` — session containers
- `TargetCard.tsx`, `SessionSummaryCard.tsx` — guided chat target + summary
- `PrepView.tsx` — pre-guided-chat translation + examples + templates + grammar

**Books / reading**
- `BookLibraryPage.tsx` — list, upload, sort, delete
- `BookReaderPage.tsx` — page view, word highlighting, selection panel, translate row
- `SelectionPanel.tsx` — save custom learning unit from selected tokens
- `SelectionReviewPanel.tsx` — review saved selections per-book. Global session loop lives at `/reading-review` (`ReadingReviewPage.tsx`, shipped #5).
- `ReadingStatsPanel.tsx` — coverage stats

**Recommendations / insights**
- `RecommendationsPanel.tsx` — For You container
- `RecommendationCards.tsx` — item/video/sentence cards
- `InsightsSection.tsx` — frequent_unknowns + recent_mistakes
- `GrammarRulePanel.tsx` — grammar rule detail + add to study

**Other**
- `PlaylistPanel.tsx`, `LoginForm.tsx`, `NotificationToast.tsx`, `ReminderBanner.tsx`, `FollowedChannelsSection.tsx`, `SettingsPanel.tsx`, `ContentRequestPage.tsx`, `SRSReviewPage.tsx`, `icons.tsx`
- `ErrorBoundary.tsx` — top-level React error boundary; reports to `api/clientErrors.ts` (W7).
- `PrivacyPage.tsx` — renders `/privacy`. Plain React, no MDX dependency. Engineering companion: `docs/PRIVACY.md`.
- `LemmaFlagButton.tsx` — learner-facing "this lemma looks wrong" flag (#39 P1).
- `AdminLemmaQueuePage.tsx` — admin review queue for lemma-correction candidates (#39 P1); gated by `useIsLemmaAdmin`.
- `WordListsPage.tsx` — vocabulary lists: paste or upload a `.txt`, see per-word known/learning/unknown/unresolved/ambiguous, mark unknown as learning, download. Route `/lists`, nav entry "Lists".

### Hooks — `src/hooks/`
| Hook | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `useSearch.ts` | Query state + pagination. Takes `language` as a parameter (defaults to `'de'`); caller threads `recLanguage`. |
| `usePreferences.ts` | Fetch + update prefs. Exposes `error`; preserves last-known prefs on failure instead of snapping back to defaults. |
| `useChat.ts`, `useGuidedChat.ts` | Free + guided chat lifecycles. |
| `usePlayerSentences.ts` | Sentence parsing, navigation. |
| `useWordStatus.ts` | Lookup + mark + W3 ambiguous-candidate flow. `recordTranscriptClick` surfaces failures via `console.warn`; backend dedups per `(user, item, sentence_id, UTC day)`. |
| `useWordColors.ts` | Page-level word-status fetch for highlighting. |
| `useNotifications.ts` | SSE consumer. |
| `useReminders.ts`, `useReadingStats.ts`, `useRecommendations.ts`, `useInsights.ts` | Feature-specific data fetchers. |
| `useResolvedTheme.ts` | Resolves the tristate `theme_mode` (`system` \| `light` \| `dark`) to a concrete theme, following `prefers-color-scheme` live when the mode is `system` (T1.3). |
| `useViewport.ts` | Returns `{ isMobile }`; the single breakpoint source for the mobile-polish work (#27a–i). |
| `useIsLemmaAdmin.ts` | Client-side admin gating for the lemma-correction queue (#39 P1). Convenience only — the server re-checks via `require_admin`. |

### API clients — `src/api/`
One file per domain, thin fetch wrappers with `assertOk`:
`account.ts`, `auth.ts`, `books.ts`, `chat.ts`, `clientErrors.ts`, `contentRequests.ts`, `insights.ts`, `lemmaCorrections.ts`, `playlists.ts`, `reading.ts`, `recommendations.ts`, `reminders.ts`, `search.ts`, `settings.ts`, `srs.ts`, `suggest.ts`, `wordLists.ts`, `words.ts`.

Two underscore-prefixed files are shared plumbing, not a domain:
- `_baseUrl.ts` — `API_BASE_URL` + `apiUrl(path)`. The single place a request URL is built, so a Capacitor/native build can point at an absolute host (see `docs/CAPACITOR_READINESS.md`).
- `_http.ts` — `assertOk` / `assertOkJson`, `detailToMessage`, and the `auth:expired` event (`AUTH_EXPIRED_EVENT` / `signalAuthExpired`) that logs the SPA out on a 401.

### Types + config + utils
- `src/types/index.ts` — every shared interface (~416 lines, single file).
- `src/config/wordColors.ts` — known/learning/unknown colour scheme.
- `src/utils/sentenceUtils.tsx` — highlightText, renderClickableText, normaliseSentences.
- `src/utils/progressUtils.ts` — formatDueDate, progressDots, PASSIVE_MAX, ACTIVE_MAX.
- `src/utils/recommendationUtils.ts` — formatDuration etc.

## Pipeline (root) — in-memory utilities, NOT mounted on FastAPI

Source files at repo root. Used by `subtitle-scraper/` and ad-hoc data prep. **This is the only pipeline tree** — the `src/app/` repackaging that used to shadow it was deleted 2026-07-27 (TODO #17). Tests live in `tests/runtime/`.

| Path | Owns |
|---|---|
| `pipeline.py` | `GermanSubtitlePipeline` orchestrator, `PipelineConfig`, `I1Match`, `parse_srt`. End-to-end SRT → i+1 matches. |
| `subtitle_cleaner.py` | `SubtitleTextCleaner`, `SubtitleFragment` — HTML/formatting removal. |
| `subtitle_merger.py` | `SubtitleMerger`, `MergedSubtitleWindow` — fragment merging, hyphenation. |
| `subtitle_segmenter.py` | `SubtitleSegmenter`, `CandidateUtterance` — sentence segmentation via spaCy. |
| ~~`subtitle_ingestion.py`~~ | **Does not exist.** SRT parsing lives in `pipeline.py` as `parse_srt` (row above). The name came from the deleted `src/app/subtitles/ingestion.py`; an earlier draft of this index listed it as if it were a root module. |
| `utterance_quality_filter.py` | `UtteranceQualityEvaluator` — length, noise, proper-noun filters. |
| `utterance_unit_extractor.py` | `UtteranceUnitExtractor` — spaCy-driven unit extraction. |
| `learning_units.py` | `LearningUnit`, `LearningUnitType` enum, lemmatisation. |
| `user_knowledge.py` | `UserKnowledgeStore`, `KnowledgeState` enum, `find_sole_unknown`, `KnowledgeFilterPolicy`. |
| `word_knowledge.py` | `WordKnowledgeStore` — lemma vocab tracking. |
| `exposure_counter.py` | `QualifiedExposureCounter`, `CountingPolicy` (ALLOW_ALL/DEDUPLICATE_UTTERANCE/DEDUPLICATE_SESSION/DIMINISHING_RETURNS). |
| `exposure_service.py` | `ExposureService` — counter + knowledge store coordination. |
| `pipeline_diagnostics.py` | `PipelineRunDiagnostics` profiling/timing. |
| `onboarding.py` | `OnboardingFlow` — tier-based vocab seeding. |
| `eligibility.py` | `EligibilityFilter` — i+1 readiness. |
| `validate_tier_lemmas.py` | One-shot B1 word list validator. |

### Tests — `tests/`
Pytest tests for pipeline modules. Mostly hermetic (no DB). 207 tests:
`tests/runtime/` (93) covers the root pipeline modules above; `tests/*.py`
(114) covers `subtitle-scraper/`. There is no longer a `src/app/` tree or
tests targeting one — see TODO #17.

## Subtitle scraper — `subtitle-scraper/`

| Path | Owns |
|---|---|
| `pipeline.py` | yt-dlp scraper orchestrator. `load_channels` (DB), `populate`, `insert_phrases`, `load_lemma_overrides` (#39 — reads `lemma_override` → patches spaCy lemma errors at phrase-generation time), `_notify_user`, `_mark_request`. Run modes: full, `--requests-only`. |
| `transcript_fetcher.py` | yt-dlp wrapper. JSON3 + WebVTT parsing. Cache under `transcript_cache/`. |
| `phrase_finder.py` | Phrase extraction dispatched by language via `extract_phrases(doc, lang)` / `_LANGUAGE_EXTRACTORS`. German (`extract_german_logic`) loads `data/final_result.txt` (path resolved from `__file__`, no chdir) + spaCy at import. Spanish (`extract_spanish_logic`, #36 slices 1+2) covers finite reflexives, an allowlisted verb+prep set, and clitic-attached reflexive infinitives, with the same output dict shape. `matcher_service.match_sentence` wraps the str in `nlp(...)` before calling the German extractor. |
| `seed_channels.py` | Bootstrap upsert of `seed_data/channels.json` → `channel` table. Idempotent. |
| `backfill_video_channels.py`, `backfill_channel_names.py`, `backfill_categories.py` | One-off backfills. |
| `channel_finder.py` | YouTube subscription / CSV channel discovery. Prints IDs to stdout. |
| `db_ssl.py` | S4 — `sslmode_from_env()` / `connect_kwargs()` resolve `DB_SSL_MODE` to a libpq `sslmode` (reject `prefer`/`allow`; omit on unset/disable). All five `psycopg2.connect()` sites pass `**connect_kwargs()`. Standalone duplicate of `database.resolve_sslmode` (scraper can't import the backend). |
| `language_config.py` | #18 — single source for per-language config (`LANGUAGES` dict: spacy_model, transcript_codes, has_morphology, phrase_extractor) + helpers + derived `MODEL_MAP`/`TRANSCRIPT_CODES`/`NO_MORPH_LANGS`. `pipeline.py` re-exports these as `LANG_*`. Add a language by editing here only. Plain Python (no YAML dep); order is load-bearing for transcript auto-detect. |
| `seed_data/channels.json` | Bundled bootstrap seed for the `channel` table. Read by `seed_channels.py`. |
| `debug_transcript.py` | Single-video transcript debug. |
| `profile_pipeline.py`, `profile_full_pipeline.py` | Profiling. |
| `top-1000-most-subscribed-youtube-channels-in-germany.csv` | Input for `channel_finder.py` CSV mode. |
| `transcript_cache/` | Cached transcripts (gitignored). |

## Data + scripts

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `data/final_result.txt` | **Central vocabulary source, and load-bearing.** 5,156-row TSV: 4,075 headwords + 950 phrase blueprints. Seeds `phrase_table` at backend startup via `main.py` → `matcher_service.get_blueprint_map()` → `phrase_service.seed_from_blueprint_map()`, and read at import by `subtitle-scraper/phrase_finder.py`. Editing it changes the live phrase catalog. |
| `data/PROVENANCE.md` | **Read before using anything in `data/`.** Per-file provenance, licence basis, measured content, and which file is preferred vs redundant. Exists because the directory previously had no attribution and an audit had to infer each file from its byte structure. |
| `data/words_4000_old.txt` | **Metadata enrichment over `final_result.txt`'s headwords, despite the name** — 11-column TSV, 4,095 entries with translations (100%), examples (100%), POS (99%), conjugations (71%), ordered frequency-descending. Joins 1:1 on the headword. `words_4000.txt` is strictly its column 0 and is redundant. |
| `data/b1_unparsed.txt`, `b1_parsed.txt` | B1 word list — unparsed is the entry set (2,840, 815 more than parsed); parsed is a strict subset adding gender + valency. Keep both. |
| `data/known_words.txt`, `verbs.txt` | Reference lists; `known_words.txt` is personal study history and is read by `tests/test_free_chat_progression.py`. |
| `files/book_pdfs/` | 29 German B1 books (PDFs). |
| `files/json/`, `files/masked/`, `files/text/` | PDF pipeline intermediates. |
| `ilp/optimal_set_finder.py` | PuLP ILP — pick minimum books covering target vocab. Standalone CLI, reads word-list files + its own DB config; outputs in same dir. **Still standalone and unchanged** — the playlist feature below re-implements the technique rather than importing this module. |
| `scripts/*.py` | One-off data fixers (verb finder, b1 word finder, known words fixer, etc.). Mostly legacy. |
| ~~`youtube_category_test/main.py`~~ | **Deleted 2026-07-27.** yt-dlp category proof-of-concept, superseded by production code: `subtitle-scraper/pipeline.py` reads `info["categories"]` on every ingest, `backfill_categories.py` fills older rows, and the result feeds `video_category` / `user_video_category`. |

## Autoloop — `autoloop/` (a SEPARATE PROJECT, not part of this codebase)

> **This section indexes a directory this repository does not maintain.**
> `autoloop/` is the autonomous engineering loop. It is a separate project that
> currently still sits in this checkout: it imports nothing from here, and what
> bound the two was shared tooling, which is now cut — `pytest.ini` does not
> collect its tests (`testpaths = tests`), `ruff.toml` excludes it from
> `ruff check .`, and `.github/workflows/tests.yml` has no job for it.
>
> **The boundary is one-directional except in exactly one place, and that place
> blocks the removal.** `scripts/seed_validation_db.py:48` does
> `from autoloop.validation_env import repo_declared_db_name` — the only file
> outside `autoloop/` here that imports the package (its row in the table below
> has said so since 2026-08-01). Neither validation command sees it: ruff does
> not resolve imports and nothing under `tests/` imports the script, so both stay
> green while it is dead. Lift it first — docs/TODO.md **#46**, and
> `docs/AUTOLOOP_REMOVAL.md`, which is the operator's removal manifest and
> survives the removal it prescribes.
>
> The per-file table below is kept only because the files are still physically
> present, so an index of this checkout would otherwise have a hole in it. It is
> **frozen**: do not extend it when the harness changes, because that work does
> not happen here and this table cannot be kept honest from this repository. It
> is removed in the same commit that removes `autoloop/` from the checkout.
>
> Two neighbours are easy to confuse with it and are **staying**:
> `.autoloop/` (leading dot) is the loop's gitignored runtime state and config
> directory inside this checkout, not the package; and `docs/audit_charters.toml`
> describes THIS repository to the loop's audit agents, so it belongs here and
> is read from here.

Infrastructure for the autonomous engineering loop.
ChatGPT is driven through a real browser (Playwright over CDP against a
dedicated, pre-logged-in Chrome profile), **never the OpenAI API**. Runtime
state in `.autoloop/` (gitignored). **Autoloop v1 (2026-07-30):**
produce-then-review (below) is the ONLY commit path — `cli.py`'s
`_build_orchestrator` always constructs the full collaborator set
(`WorkerRepoManager`, `TaskExecutionStore`, `IntentStore`, a provisioned
`Publisher`); the older authorize-then-produce/change-manifest path was
retired, not fixed (`docs/SECURITY.md` S21). There is now a real
write-capable executor (`implement_executor.py`) alongside the audit — both
run through the same path.

**Worker-isolation hardening (2026-07-31, `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4e,
`docs/SECURITY.md` S23-S26):** an adversarial review of the M2 worker
isolation (below) found it was git-configuration isolation only —
filesystem location and task-scope authorization were unaddressed. Closed:
worker repos now live at a required, externally-validated
`config.workers_root` instead of inside the checkout; a task's write scope
(`Task.approved_paths`) is fixed before the writer starts and can never be
widened by anything the executor reports; a failed round's residue is
quarantined rather than silently reused (since wrk-01, 2026-08-18, only
for preparations that did NOT pass the resumed-worker reuse gate — a valid
recorded worker is resumed as it stands, residue included, and the
residual-dirty refusal at post-commit review is what still keeps
unreported content out of a candidate); the attempt-count ceiling
persists before the executor runs, not after a commit. Opened, honestly: a
new filesystem-snapshot escape DETECTOR (`escape_detector.py`) catches an
agent writing outside its worker repo, but detection is not an OS-level
sandbox — recorded as open finding S24, on purpose.

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `orchestrator.py` | Persisted state machine: ready → (**delivering**) → submitting → awaiting → executing (+ submission_unconfirmed / **submission_rejected** / needs_user / stopped / failed). **Chunked packet delivery (2026-08-14, pkt-01):** `delivering` is entered only when a review packet's patch is too large for one chat message; it deposits the patch as numbered parts, confirming each by READBACK from persisted history (mounting the virtualized message tail first, when the provider offers it), and reaches `submitting` only once every part is confirmed — `_step_submitting` re-checks that itself and raises rather than requesting a verdict on a partial patch. Any part that does not land, or a provider that does not declare `supports_chunked_delivery`, falls back to the omission notice via `_fall_back_to_omission`, which re-stamps all three holders of `report_sha256` (request, `postcommit.packet_sha256`, `TaskExecution.presented_report_sha256`) and disowns the parts that already landed. **Transport recovery (2026-07-31):** a send whose acceptance is merely unobserved still licenses nothing; a send the browser *disproves* routes to `submission_rejected`, which reconciles for confirmation and, on confirmed absence, permits exactly ONE same-chat resend of the same request id. A second confirmed rejection — or a `ConversationUnusableError` — may rotate once (`policy.max_conversation_rotations`, `browser.project_url` required, no derivation) via submit-then-capture-then-**reconcile**: the new URL binds nothing until the conversation itself confirms it holds the request. Every request carries its own authoritative `conversation_url` + `conversation_epoch`, so a late reply in an abandoned chat can never authorize anything. **False ambiguity (2026-08-16):** before parking `submission_ambiguous`, `_resolve_or_park_ambiguous` asks the by-content search (`find_conversation_with`, which mounts the virtualized tail) whether the request is actually there — the 2026-08-05 park on `alr-af11e1b3-0006`, whose request was present and already answered. Proof of presence in this request's OWN conversation (`_same_conversation`, compared by `/c/<id>` because the search's `urljoin` drops the project prefix) resumes into `awaiting` and sends nothing; absence, a hit in a different chat, a refusal to conclude, or no project configured all park exactly as before, saying which. Presence is proved and acted on; absence is never inferred and never acted on, and `run --resubmit` stays the only thing that repeats a send. Failure routing, budgets, review-integrity enforcement. `_dispatch_executor` ALWAYS routes to `_dispatch_task_postcommit` (the produce-then-review commit path) — audit included, via `_resolve_audit_task`'s synthetic per-run `Task` (a stable unit id distinct from the protocol pseudo-id `"audit"`, so a `revise` round resumes the same worker repo instead of forking a new one). The legacy manifest-based authorize-then-produce branch and `_dispatch_git` are REMOVED (S21); a stray `commit`/`commit_and_push`/unbound `push` directive is refused via the ordinary `policy_denied` corrective-reprompt machinery (`legacy_git_path_retired`), never routed to an executor. On success the commit is created immediately (hooks enabled) and gated on structural + re-run-validation checks, never a prior chat approval; ANY failure parks in `needs_user` with the commit left exactly where it is (nothing here can roll it back). **Publisher (Autoloop M2, wired 2026-07-30):** `publisher`/`publisher_url_snapshot` constructor params — `_dispatch_task_push` imports the candidate into the dedicated `Publisher` repo and publishes from there, refusing BEFORE publishing if the main checkout's live `remote.origin.url` no longer matches the provisioned snapshot (naming `reprovision-publisher --confirm`) — see `publisher.py`. **Blockers (2026-07-31):** every `_to_needs_user` park is now classified `kind="task_fatal"` or `kind="loop_fatal"` (default `loop_fatal`, fail-closed — see `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §9c) and, when `blocker_store` is configured (always true in production), persisted as a `blockers.Blocker`. **Worker-isolation hardening (2026-07-31, `docs/SECURITY.md` S24/S25):** for a non-audit write-capable dispatch, `_prepare_write_capable_worker` requires the primary checkout clean (`primary_checkout_dirty`, loop_fatal) and the worker repo free of residue from a failed prior attempt (quarantines + recreates otherwise), then re-checks `Task.approved_paths` for symlink traversal (`approved_path_symlink_traversal`, task_fatal); `_execute_with_escape_detection` brackets the executor call with `escape_detector`'s before/after checkout snapshot (`checkout_escape_detected`, loop_fatal); `attempt_count` increments and persists before the executor is ever called, not in `_finish_postcommit` anymore. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4e for the full design. **Path scope is ADVISORY at both gates (2026-08-05, operator decision — `docs/SECURITY.md` S25 amendment):** the pre-commit check (`outcome.changed_paths` vs `effective_approved_paths`) and the post-commit one in `_verify_committed` (`commit_range_paths` vs `execution.allowed_paths`) both still run the same `tasks.unauthorized_paths` matcher on the same inputs, but now RECORD onto `TaskExecution.out_of_scope_paths` and let the round reach review instead of parking (`changed_paths_outside_approved` is retired as a park code; both had to change together, since relaxing only the first moves the park downstream). Recorded from the comparison, never from an agent claim — `allowed_paths` is still never widened. NOT relaxed: an empty `approved_paths` still refuses dispatch (`approved_paths_missing`), escape detection is still loop_fatal, and every other post-commit refusal stands. **Fault stop (auto-04, 2026-08-16):** an exhausted POLICY-denial budget no longer parks — `_handle_policy_denial` calls the new `_to_fault_stop`, which ends the run in `stopped` with `stop_kind="fault"` while still recording the same `loop_fatal` blocker. A park asks a human a question, and there is none: only the reviewer that just spent the budget could produce an acceptable directive, which is why this was the last park a retired `ask_user` could still reach (by being re-answered). The two sibling sites spending the same `state.policy_denials` counter — `_dispatch_plan`'s rejected plan and `_handle_review_mismatch` — deliberately still park, each with a comment saying why (an operator-owned roadmap; a repository that may have moved under the loop). `_dispatch`'s `stop` branch now classifies itself `"contract"`, so no reader has to infer "clean" from the phase. **A throttled account vs an unattachable browser (brw-11, 2026-08-17):** `_handle_rate_limited` classifies before it waits and before it concludes the limit still holds (`_classify_rate_limit_state`). A throttle modal on a page the loop can drive behaves exactly as before — no restart, no client drop, no `consecutive_failures` — and is asked FIRST, so a genuine throttle is never answered by the restart that deepens it. No modal plus a real click that LANDS (`chatgpt.composer_interactive`) resumes without waiting, still counting the occurrence so a page that keeps clearing cannot become an immediate-retry loop. Zero attachable page targets (`playwright_session.attachable_page_targets`) is NOT a rate limit — the operator closed the window, Chrome is alive, `/json/version` answers and there is no page to be throttled — so `_recover_unattachable_browser` drops the client, restarts the profile ONCE, re-probes once, and otherwise parks `browser_unattachable` naming the BROWSER. That restart spends no budget at all: `rate_limit_backoffs` bounds waiting on the SERVER, and this makes no request. It is bounded per EPISODE instead (`_rate_limit_browser_restarted`), and an episode ends where the throttle streak does — on a step that COMPLETES, cleared unconditionally in `run`'s `else` branch rather than inside the `rate_limit_backoffs` reset, since state 3 never increments that counter and a guard nested there would stay true for the rest of the process. **The restart boundary (loop-02, 2026-08-18):** `_self_upgrade_due` + the `SELF_UPGRADE` outcome. When `auto_merge` has recorded that a merge changed `autoloop/`, `run()` ends the round — after the terminal-phase check (a parked loop reports its park) and before the step budget — at `ready` with no `pending_request`, i.e. immediately before `_step_ready` prepares the next request: nothing sent, nothing awaited, no agent writing (that is the `executing` phase, synchronously), the payload already durable in `state.outbox`. Every other phase is mid-round by construction and is refused, and `pending_request` is checked separately because it outlives its own phase. This class reads the record and never writes it and never execs; the replacement is `cli._self_upgrade_at_boundary`'s. The boundary is opt-in per construction (`self_upgrade_enabled`, default OFF, set only by `cli._build_orchestrator`): the record lives under `state_dir`, and `smoke-browser` builds its own orchestrator in the boundary shape (`ready`, no pending request) while reporting PASS only for a clean contract stop, so an unrelated pending upgrade would fail a diagnostic that is diagnosing something else. A send that never appears is the conversation, not the browser (brw-12, 2026-08-18):** `_handle_conversation_unusable` now carries the error's `code` into the transcript and the `RotationRecord` reason, so a rotation forced by a vanished submission (`submission_never_appeared`, raised by the transport's mounted-tail proof during `awaiting`) stays distinguishable from a chat that would not load — and, like every shape of that error, it bypasses the restart machinery and never charges `consecutive_failures`. — EXCEPT when the dispatch already passed the wrk-01 resumed-worker reuse gate, 2026-08-18: `reused_recorded_worker=True` skips only the residue quarantine, because a valid recorded worker's uncommitted residue is the interrupted round's own resumable work; `_verify_committed`'s residual-dirty refusal still keeps unreported content out of any candidate), then re-checks `Task.approved_paths` for symlink traversal (`approved_path_symlink_traversal`, task_fatal); `_execute_with_escape_detection` brackets the executor call with `escape_detector`'s before/after checkout snapshot (`checkout_escape_detected`, loop_fatal); `attempt_count` increments and persists before the executor is ever called, not in `_finish_postcommit` anymore. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4e for the full design. **Path scope is ADVISORY at both gates (2026-08-05, operator decision — `docs/SECURITY.md` S25 amendment):** the pre-commit check (`outcome.changed_paths` vs `effective_approved_paths`) and the post-commit one in `_verify_committed` (`commit_range_paths` vs `execution.allowed_paths`) both still run the same `tasks.unauthorized_paths` matcher on the same inputs, but now RECORD onto `TaskExecution.out_of_scope_paths` and let the round reach review instead of parking (`changed_paths_outside_approved` is retired as a park code; both had to change together, since relaxing only the first moves the park downstream). Recorded from the comparison, never from an agent claim — `allowed_paths` is still never widened. NOT relaxed: an empty `approved_paths` still refuses dispatch (`approved_paths_missing`), escape detection is still loop_fatal, and every other post-commit refusal stands. **Fault stop (auto-04, 2026-08-16):** an exhausted POLICY-denial budget no longer parks — `_handle_policy_denial` calls the new `_to_fault_stop`, which ends the run in `stopped` with `stop_kind="fault"` while still recording the same `loop_fatal` blocker. A park asks a human a question, and there is none: only the reviewer that just spent the budget could produce an acceptable directive, which is why this was the last park a retired `ask_user` could still reach (by being re-answered). The two sibling sites spending the same `state.policy_denials` counter — `_dispatch_plan`'s rejected plan and `_handle_review_mismatch` — deliberately still park, each with a comment saying why (an operator-owned roadmap; a repository that may have moved under the loop). `_dispatch`'s `stop` branch now classifies itself `"contract"`, so no reader has to infer "clean" from the phase. **A throttled account vs an unattachable browser (brw-11, 2026-08-17):** `_handle_rate_limited` classifies before it waits and before it concludes the limit still holds (`_classify_rate_limit_state`). A throttle modal on a page the loop can drive behaves exactly as before — no restart, no client drop, no `consecutive_failures` — and is asked FIRST, so a genuine throttle is never answered by the restart that deepens it. No modal plus a real click that LANDS (`chatgpt.composer_interactive`) resumes without waiting, still counting the occurrence so a page that keeps clearing cannot become an immediate-retry loop. Zero attachable page targets (`playwright_session.attachable_page_targets`) is NOT a rate limit — the operator closed the window, Chrome is alive, `/json/version` answers and there is no page to be throttled — so `_recover_unattachable_browser` drops the client, restarts the profile ONCE, re-probes once, and otherwise parks `browser_unattachable` naming the BROWSER. That restart spends no budget at all: `rate_limit_backoffs` bounds waiting on the SERVER, and this makes no request. It is bounded per EPISODE instead (`_rate_limit_browser_restarted`), and an episode ends where the throttle streak does — on a step that COMPLETES, cleared unconditionally in `run`'s `else` branch rather than inside the `rate_limit_backoffs` reset, since state 3 never increments that counter and a guard nested there would stay true for the rest of the process. |
| `tasks.py`, `seed_tasks.json` | Task registry / graph: stable slug ids, dependencies, derived ready/blocked (never stored), `next_ready()`, cycle detection, atomic `tasks.json` persistence. ChatGPT authorizes work by task id only. `seed_tasks.json` (git-tracked) seeds a fresh registry with `rt-01` (admin-gate the books-import endpoints) whenever `tasks.json` doesn't exist yet — read-only, consulted by `cli._load_tasks`/`next-task`/continuous mode's selection policy. **Blockers (2026-07-31):** `block(task_id, reason)`/`unblock(task_id)` quarantine/restore a task via a new `status="blocked"` + `blocked_reason` field, surfaced as `TaskState.BLOCKED_BY_OPERATOR` (distinct from the dependency-derived `BLOCKED`) so `ready_tasks()`/`next_ready()` skip it with no change to either method. **`Task.approved_paths`, added 2026-07-31** (`docs/SECURITY.md` S25): the EXACT repo-relative paths a task's write-capable dispatch may touch — validated on the way into `add_many` (`_validate_approved_path`: no globs, no `..`, not absolute/home-relative, `TaskGraphError` code `bad_approved_path`/`duplicate_approved_path`), defaulted to `()` for backward compatibility with an old `tasks.json`. `seed_tasks.json`'s `rt-01` now declares its three real paths (`lexy-app/backend/routers/books.py`, `lexy-app/backend/tests/test_books_import_admin.py`, `docs/SECURITY.md`); `_seed_registry` also now correctly threads `validation`/`validation_cwd` through (a pre-existing gap — they were declared in the JSON but silently dropped before this pass). **`TRACKER_PATHS` / `effective_approved_paths`, added 2026-08-01:** the four `CLAUDE.md`-mandated trackers (`docs/SUMMARY.md`, `docs/TESTS.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/COMMON_ERRORS.md`) are IMPLICITLY approved for every SCOPED task, because updating them is a condition of doing the work — a task that adds a file and does not name `SUMMARY.md` cannot be completed while obeying the repo's own rules. Combined in exactly one place so the dispatch seed, the every-dispatch re-sync, the PRE-commit gate and the POST-commit ownership check cannot drift. An UNSCOPED task stays unscoped (finding #2 unchanged). See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4f-bis. **`TaskState.RETIRED` + `Task.superseded_by`, added 2026-08-16 (task-retire-01):** the THIRD meaning `status="blocked"` was carrying — superseded work, which resolves for nobody, as against `BLOCKED` (a dependency will resolve it) and `BLOCKED_BY_OPERATOR` (a human will). `retire(task_id, superseded_by=(), reason="")` sets `status="retired"` and records the successor id(s); it accepts a pending, in-progress or quarantined task and refuses only a completed one, and it DELETES NOTHING — `blocked_reason` survives untouched, and there is no un-retire. `_validate_superseded_by` checks shape only (valid slug, no self-reference, no repeats): a successor need not exist, since brw-06 was split into brw-07 + brw-08 before either was planned, and nothing schedules off the field. `_RETIREMENTS` + `_migrate_retirements` re-file the six pre-state retirements (brw-02, brw-04, brw-05, brw-06, sub-01, dash-01) when `from_dict` loads them — a code-resident data migration because `tasks.json` is loop state outside this repository — guarded on BOTH the stored status and a marker still present in the reason, so it is idempotent and cannot sweep up `audit-0003`, the one genuine failure. `python -m autoloop retire` is the operator route and the fallback for a reason that no longer matches. A retirement is written ONCE: a second `retire` that would add, replace, reorder or reword anything raises `task_already_retired`, and an exact repeat is a no-op — omitting `--superseded-by` on a repeat used to assign `()` over the recorded chain, making the command the thing that deleted the record. `_persisted_superseded_by` runs the same validator over the field as read back off `tasks.json`, since `from_dict` bypasses `add_many` and is the only gate a stored row passes; a malformed chain is `StateCorruptError`, not a bare string loading as one successor per character. `block` refuses a retired task too — it ends in a bare `status = "blocked"` that would otherwise silently un-retire the row. **Operator mutators, added 2026-08-16 (inbox-02):** `set_approved_paths`, `set_depends_on`, `operator_block`, `operator_unblock` join `set_priority`/`set_description` as the registry side of `inbox.py`'s mutation vocabulary. All three content mutators (description, approved_paths, depends_on) route through `_refuse_immutable`, which allows only `pending`/`blocked` — an `in_progress` task's fields are what a live dispatch is being judged against (a new dependency makes it fail BOTH `mark_completed` and `release`; a swapped scope makes its own writes read as unauthorized), and `completed`/`retired` are records, not queue. Guarded on the STORED status, never `state_of()`, which reports BLOCKED for an in-progress task with an incomplete dependency and would fall silent on exactly the stranded case. Validation is shared with creation: `_validate_approved_paths` and `_validate_depends_on` were extracted from `add_many`'s inline loops (the duplicate-path and known-dependency rules lived nowhere else) and both now also check SHAPE, so a bare string no longer splits per character. `set_depends_on` runs `_check_acyclic` against a `replace()`d candidate, so a refused cycle never touches the registry. `operator_block`/`operator_unblock` are a separate pair from `block`/`unblock`: `block`'s own caller (`cli._handle_parked_task`) quarantines a task that is by definition in-progress and fail-closes to loop_fatal on refusal, so the strand guard cannot live there. `operator_block` refuses an already-blocked task and records the hold's provenance in the new **`Task.hold_origin`** field (`HOLD_ORIGIN_OPERATOR`); `operator_unblock` releases ONLY a task carrying that origin, so an inbox request cannot clear a `task_fatal` quarantine with its blocker still open (that stays `python -m autoloop answer`). Provenance is a stored value and deliberately NOT the `blocked_reason` text: that field is unconstrained free text loop-raised quarantines write too, so the first cut's `blocked_reason.startswith(OPERATOR_HOLD_PREFIX)` test let a genuine quarantine whose reason merely began with those characters be released from the inbox. `block()` clears `hold_origin` unconditionally (whatever the reason says, and so an idempotent re-block cannot inherit a marker), `unblock()` clears it on release, `operator_block` is the only writer and sets it AFTER the delegate returns (so a refused hold stamps nothing), and `from_dict` coerces a missing/`null` value to `""` — an unmarked stored row reads as a loop quarantine, the safe direction. `OPERATOR_HOLD_PREFIX` survives as PROSE on the reason for whoever reads the row and decides nothing. See `docs/SECURITY.md` S30. **`TRACKER_PATHS` stays a fixed constant (2026-08-16, port-02):** it gained a `trackers` PARAMETER on `effective_approved_paths(approved, trackers=TRACKER_PATHS)`, fed in production only by `Orchestrator._tracker_paths()`, which returns the constant — no config read. A `[repo].tracker_paths` setting was written and withdrawn in review the same day: the config lives under the gitignored state dir, so an edit to it is not a reviewed diff, and the "documentation only" suffix blocklist offered as its bound cannot hold (`.env`, `.gitignore`, `Makefile`, `Dockerfile`, extensionless scripts all pass it, and the set is open-ended). `validate_tracker_paths` / `_NON_TRACKER_SUFFIXES` were deleted rather than left caller-less. Portability comes from the constant itself: `autoloop/` is vendored into the repo it operates on, so editing it in a target repo is a commit in that repo's reviewed history. An unscoped task still gains nothing for any tracker list, including an empty one. See `docs/SECURITY.md` S31 and `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4f-bis. **The task file is now written by two processes, and coordinated (2026-08-16, dash-04):** `task_file_mutex` is a SHORT-LIVED mutex (a `flock` on `tasks.json.lock`, re-entrant per thread, `TaskStoreBusy` on timeout) held for the milliseconds of one load/mutate/save — deliberately not `LoopLock`, which is held for the whole run and so cannot serialise an operator edit against a live loop. `TaskStore.save`/`archive` and the new `TaskStore.apply_priority` (the dashboard's immediate write, returning the task READ BACK from disk) all take it. `save` also reconciles: it adopts the on-disk `priority` for every task the caller has not itself re-prioritised (`TaskRegistry.priority_overrides`, populated by `set_priority` and cleared on save), so a loop holding a registry in memory for a whole round cannot write a mid-round operator edit back out, while a drained inbox request still wins. Reconciliation fails OPEN on an unreadable file. `MutationLedger` + `mutation_ledger_for` record each immediate write as a `before -> after` content-digest hop in a file beside `workers_root` — outside the checkout, like the inbox and the PAUSE flag — and `attested_priority_edit` (`MutationLedger.completed_chain` + `priority_only_change`, which re-reads the bytes) is what lets the escape detector tell that edit from an agent writing into the state dir. **The attestation is a CHAIN, not reachability (tightened 2026-08-16 in review):** each write appends TWO records — `record_intent` before the file write (so an unwritable ledger leaves the task file untouched) and `record_complete` after it (the only phase that is ever an edge) — and `completed_chain(start, since=watermark, tasks_path=…)` returns the sequence of states the window's completed mutations walked through, or `None` if they do not chain unbroken. `attested_priority_edit` demands that chain's TERMINAL state be exactly what the detector observed, which is what closes two holes reachability left open: a legitimate round trip (3 → 2 → 3) used to leave its intermediate digest usable forever, and a record written before a write that then FAILED used to be an edge to a state that never existed on disk. `canonical_task_path` binds a record to one task file (a shared ledger must not attest another checkout's edits) and `TaskStore.priority_edit_watermark` / `capture_priority_window` bind it to one detection window — the latter reading the baseline bytes and the watermark under a single mutex hold, since taken apart they can straddle an in-flight edit and park a benign one. See `docs/SECURITY.md` S32 and `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4f-ter. **`Task.decomposition` + `set_decomposition` (plan-01, 2026-08-18):** where an approved decomposition becomes durable. Text, not a step list — it is agent instructions in the same category as `description`, and a machine-actionable list here would be a second way to split a task (that stays `split-01`'s). Written only from the dispatch path, in the same save as `mark_in_progress`, so a task is never in progress against an unrecorded plan; `policy._check_decomposition` reads it to authorize a later `implement`, and `implement_executor._agent_prompt` shows it to the agent. REPLACES on a reshape, refuses blank (empty means "nothing approved yet", the state dispatch is refused from) and refuses `completed`/`retired`. Deliberately NOT behind `_refuse_immutable`: an `in_progress` task must stay reshapeable, because a task under review is in progress by definition and the write happens before that round's executor starts, judged against by nothing. A `tasks.json` written before the field existed loads with `""`. |
| `execution_records.py` | **New 2026-08-26 (shrink-01), extracted from `orchestrator.py` unchanged.** Execution-record lifecycle: `release_task_to_pending` — THE release path, shared by `cli._cmd_release`, the urgent preemption, the reviewer's recut, the ceiling split and the autonomous stale-record rebuild — the `Release` it returns, and the six helpers only it calls (`_archived_record_path`, `_loaded_execution`, `_worker_can_resume`, `_repair_orphaned_record`, `_surviving_worker_path`, `_surviving_execution_record`). `orchestrator.py` imports both public names back, so `orchestrator.release_task_to_pending` and `orchestrator.Release` still resolve and no caller moved. Behaviour, order and signatures are identical; see `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4f-quinquies for what the path does. |
| `changeset_review.py` | Operator-changeset review: binds a stamped ChatGPT approval to a commit an **operator** authored directly on the branch — never one this loop's executor produced. |
| `heartbeat.py` | The loop's published liveness, readable without touching the checkout (`health.check` answers better but must read the state dir, blocker store and transcript). |
| `path_suggest.py` | Suggests the files a task will probably touch from its own words, so `approved_paths` is not hand-typed or copied from a neighbouring task. Feeds the intake draft (§4f-sexies); the human submit is still what authorizes the scope. |
| `blockers.py` | **New 2026-07-31.** Persisted operator-facing `Blocker` records (`id`, `task_id`, `kind`, `code`, `question`, `detail`, `phase`, `created_at`, `resolved_at`, `answer`) — one JSON file per blocker under `state_dir/blockers/`, atomic temp-file + `os.replace` writes, a corrupt record RAISES (`StateCorruptError`) rather than reading as absent. `BlockerStore.next_id(task_id)` derives `blk-<task_id>-<NNN>` by scanning the directory (no separate counter to drift). `resolve(id, answer)` is one-way — refuses an unknown or already-resolved id. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §9c. |
| `conversation.py` | `LLMConversation` abstract interface + provider registry. Two built in since 2026-08-01: **`codex_cli`** (the default reviewer — one `codex exec` process per turn) and `browser_chatgpt` (Playwright over CDP). A further adapter is one class + `register_provider`. Two capabilities are OPTIONAL and probed with `getattr`, so an adapter implementing only the protocol stays valid: send observation (`SubmitResult.REJECTED`), rotation (`retarget`/`current_url`), and `idempotent_submit` (a failed send left nothing behind, so a retry cannot double-post). |
| `codex/conversation.py`, `codex/quota.py` | **Added 2026-08-01.** The Codex CLI reviewer. A subprocess returns an exit code, so none of the browser transport's ambiguity machinery is reachable: `submit` runs the CLI and stashes the reply (CONFIRMED or REJECTED, **never** UNCONFIRMED), `await_response` returns the stash, and `reconcile` is authoritative because the transport is synchronous. Declares `idempotent_submit`; omits `retarget`/`current_url`, so rotation is structurally unreachable rather than disabled. Runs with `cwd` **outside the checkout** — the prompt is self-contained, so the reviewer needs no filesystem access, and that containment holds without depending on a sandbox flag's name. `quota.py` is a pure `(returncode, stdout, stderr) -> bool` classifier with configurable patterns: the real exhaustion wording could not be verified here, so every non-zero exit logs its return code and a bounded stderr tail, making the first real exhaustion a config edit rather than an investigation. |
| `conversation.py` | **2026-08-22 (codex-01).** A THIRD provider is registered: `codex_app_server`, alongside `codex_cli` and `browser_chatgpt`. Nothing is replaced — `_codex_cli_factory` is untouched and `conversation.fallback_provider` can still name any of the three. The `supports_chunked_delivery` note in the module docstring gained the case that makes the capability real for a codex seat: a transport holding ONE THREAD may declare it, and this one does. |
| `codex/app_server.py`, `codex/app_server_conversation.py`, `codex/wire.py`, `codex/protocol_errors.py` | **Added 2026-08-22 (codex-01).** The Codex app-server reviewer — `codex app-server` driven as a subprocess over newline-delimited JSON-RPC on stdio, no SDK package (there is no `codex_sdk`; `@openai/codex-sdk` is TypeScript) and stdlib only. **What it buys:** one thread. `thread/start` once, `turn/start` per turn, so an oversized diff reaches the reviewer as numbered parts and the verdict question reaches it afterwards **on the same thread within one `submit`** — which is why this adapter DOES declare `supports_chunked_delivery` where `codex/conversation.py` must not. `reconcile` is an authority rather than a memory: it calls `thread/read` and answers True only when the request is on the thread AND has an agent reply after it, stashing the reply it finds. **`idempotent_submit = False`, explicitly:** `turn/start` appends its input and then runs, so a failure afterwards may leave the prompt on the thread and a retry would double-post; the ambiguity is resolved from the server instead of by declaration. **Errors are objects:** `protocol_errors.py` classifies from named fields (`error.code`, `data.type`/`code`/`kind`/`status`) with exact matches and a numeric 429, never a substring scan — the counter-case is the point, since an error whose PROSE mentions a usage limit but whose type is unknown routes as an ordinary failure. Vocabulary overridable via `codex.quota_error_codes`. **`wire.py` splits pinned from unpinned:** every method and notification name is checked against `docs/codex-app-server-protocol.generated.ts` by test, while the `v2/` param bodies are referenced-but-not-concatenated there, so those field spellings are tolerant candidate lists that return None rather than a plausible wrong answer. **Containment, stated only as far as it is provable:** the server runs with `cwd` outside the checkout, stderr goes to DEVNULL, and every approval it requests is answered `{"decision": "abort"}` while any other server→client request gets a JSON-RPC error (silence would wedge the turn). **Deliberately absent:** `thread/resume` is never sent (no restart durability) and no sandbox preset is selected or enforced — both are codex-03. |
| `config.py` | **2026-08-22 (codex-01).** `[codex]` gained four app-server keys — `app_server_command`, `app_server_part_chars`, `app_server_max_attachment_chars`, `quota_error_codes` — and the loader's list→tuple coercion list gained the two tuple-typed ones. That list is load-bearing and easy to miss: `_check_keys` reflects over the dataclass and accepts a new key automatically, so a tuple field omitted from the coercion loop lands in the frozen dataclass as a mutable list with its element types unchecked. The `codex_cli` half of the section is untouched. |
| `codex/quota.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** Rewritten around one rule: the prompt is not evidence. `codex exec` echoes the WHOLE prompt onto stderr (measured: 180,024 bytes came back verbatim), so the old `f"{stdout}\n{stderr}"` haystack contained the entire review packet, and with `"429"`, `"quota"` and `"rate limit"` in the list as bare substrings a packet quoting a LINE NUMBER (`docs/autoloop.md:4295`) made any non-zero exit read as a spent allowance — the loop parked twice, loop_fatal, on 2026-08-22 against an account 4% through its weekly window. `classify(returncode, stdout, stderr, prompt, ...)` takes the prompt as a REQUIRED argument (no default, so a call site cannot silently disable the guard) and counts a marker only when it does NOT occur in what was sent. A GUARD rather than an attempt to carve the echo out of the stream: nothing recognises the echo's shape, so reframing, indenting or reflowing it cannot reopen the hole, and `codex.quota_patterns` is safe to widen again. Two lists now, not one — `DEFAULT_QUOTA_PATTERNS` (a SPENT allowance, tested first) and `DEFAULT_RATE_LIMIT_PATTERNS` (a transient throttle, which is where `"429"` and `"too many requests"` moved) — because only the first may reach the loop_fatal `QuotaExhaustedError`. `returncode == 0` is checked in front of BOTH. Empty/blank patterns are dropped (`_usable`): the empty string is a substring of everything, so one blank config entry would classify every failure as exhaustion. `strip_echoed_prompt` + `failure_digest` are the SEPARATE, conservative bound for diagnostics — exact prompt occurrences are excised and text on BOTH sides is kept, because real stderr is `Error: <diagnostic>` then the echo and a strip that keeps only what follows the echo returns nothing. The digest carries the return code, the classification, the deciding marker, the markers the guard suppressed, bounded excerpts of BOTH streams and their raw sizes — and says so explicitly when codex printed only an echo, or nothing. |
| `codex/conversation.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** `submit` classifies with `quota.classify` and passes the FINAL prompt (after any attachment is inlined), which is the guard; it logs `codex_invocation_failed` with the full digest on every non-zero exit AND on a clean exit with no reply, carrying `request_id`. Only a SPENT allowance raises `QuotaExhaustedError` (loop_fatal, no retry path); a transient throttle and an unrecognised fault both return `SubmitResult.REJECTED`, which is the retryable path this transport already had. `rate_limit_patterns` joins `quota_patterns` on the constructor. The adapter deliberately does NOT raise `RateLimitedError`: that error routes through `orchestrator._classify_rate_limit_state`, which probes a browser page and counts CDP targets, and a closed Chrome window (zero targets, `/json/version` still answering) parks `loop_fatal` on `browser_unattachable` — one false park traded for another. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §5d. |
| `conversation.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** `_transcript_log(config)` — the production failure logger every codex adapter is now built with. `CodexConversation`, `CodexAppServerConversation` and `AppServerClient` all take `log=` and all default it to a no-op, and all three factories constructed them without passing anything, so `codex_invocation_failed` appeared ZERO times in 24 days of transcript despite being written on every failure since the adapter shipped. Fixed at the construction site rather than by removing the default: a no-op is right for a unit test and wrong only in production. Not wrapped in `try` — `orchestrator._log` is not either, and a transcript that cannot be written is a real fault, not a record that quietly does not exist. |
| `config.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** `[codex]` gained `rate_limit_patterns` (transient throttle markers, kept apart from `quota_patterns` so a thirty-second 429 never reaches the permanent branch) and the loader's list→tuple coercion list gained it too — the same load-bearing list the codex-01 row above describes. |
| `codex/protocol_errors.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** The same transient-vs-spent split, one transport over, because this module had the same defect the stderr matcher did: `rate_limit_exceeded`, `rate_limited`, `too_many_requests` and a numeric `429` were all in the EXHAUSTION vocabulary, so a short-window throttle raised the loop_fatal `QuotaExhaustedError` from a structured field instead of from a substring. Two vocabularies now — `DEFAULT_QUOTA_ERROR_CODES` (spent) and `DEFAULT_RATE_LIMIT_ERROR_CODES` (throttle), with `RATE_LIMIT_STATUS_CODES = (429,)` — plus `is_rate_limited` and `classification_of`, which is the ONE place the spent-beats-transient precedence lives so the transcript record and the raised exception cannot disagree. A throttle returns `CodexProtocolError(transient=True)`, already retryable because it is a `BrowserError`; an unrecognised failure returns the same class and is never swallowed. `failure_digest` gained `classification`. `RateLimitedError` is deliberately not raised, for the reason the `codex/conversation.py` row above gives. The honest limit is stated rather than engineered around: there is no prompt guard here (the comparison is exact against a named field, never against printed text), so an operator who files a throttle code in `codex.quota_error_codes` is obeyed and parks the loop. |
| `codex/app_server.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** `AppServerClient` takes `rate_limit_codes` beside `quota_codes`, both falling back through the new `protocol_errors.usable_codes`, which tests the list's CONTENTS rather than its length — the usual `tuple(codes) or DEFAULT` idiom passes a configured `[""]` straight through, and the client then recognises no error at all, which is a check switched off by config. Equality matching means a blank could never over-classify (the opposite of the substring hole `quota._usable` closes); the harm would be a real exhaustion going unrecognised. `_raise` passes both to `classify` and to `failure_digest`. The digest is now built from the SAME object `classify` reads rather than from a reshaped `{"message": str(error)}` copy for a non-`dict`, so `codex_app_server_failed` cannot name a different route from the one the exception took. |
| `config.py` | **2026-08-23 (quota-01).** `[codex]` gained `rate_limit_error_codes`, the app-server twin of `rate_limit_patterns`, and the loader's list→tuple coercion list gained it — the same list the two rows above warn about. `autoloop/config.example.toml` documents both error-code keys for the first time; the three `app_server_*` size/command keys are still undocumented there (`docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §5d-ter names that gap). |
| `blockers.py` | **2026-08-25 (halt-02).** Autonomous recovery's TABLE lives here: `HARD_HALT_CODES` (the five parks nothing may automate), `AUTONOMOUS_RECOVERIES` (code → action + retry budget + the argument for each), `autonomous_recovery(code)` (refuses a hard halt before consulting the table, so the allowlist cannot be bypassed by a caller that only remembers the dict), `BlockerStore.open_recurrences(task, code)` (the retry meter — sums across every OPEN record for the pair, because `record`/`find_open` key on phase and a fault that migrates one phase along would otherwise buy a second allowance) and `BlockerStore.close_recovered` (closes a recovered fault with `archived_reason`, never `answer`, the same discipline `archive_stale` follows). In this module rather than `orchestrator.py` because it is data about blocker codes, and because that module's source is read as evidence by `test_transport_recovery.py`'s retired-code walk. |
| `config.py` | **2026-08-25 (halt-02).** New `[autonomy]` section — `AutonomyConfig(enabled=False, max_recovery_attempts=2)`, added to `_SECTIONS` and to `AutoloopConfig` with a default, so every direct construction and every pre-existing config file keeps the old parking behaviour without naming it. The loader TYPE-CHECKS both keys rather than coercing: `enabled = "true"` is a `ConfigError`, because a truthy string must not read as consent to run without an operator, and `max_recovery_attempts` rejects `bool` before `int` since `True` would otherwise read as a budget of 1. `max_recovery_attempts` is a CEILING on the table's own numbers, never a floor. |
| `orchestrator.py` | **2026-08-25 (halt-02).** `_to_needs_user` is the ONE interception point for autonomous recovery: it resolves the set-aside task BEFORE writing the blocker record (`record` upserts on (task, code, phase) and never rewrites `kind`/`task_id`, so one identity has to be decided once), then either re-enters the recovery path (`_autonomous_retry` → `_resumable_phase`, or a same-id re-issue) or parks `task_fatal` naming that task. `run`'s completed-step branch gained `_close_recovered_blocker`, beside the rate-limit reset and on the same evidence. `cli.py`, `state.py`, `policy.py` and `tasks.py` are untouched — the set-aside reuses `cli._handle_parked_task`'s existing quarantine by writing `state.park_kind`/`park_task_id`, which is all that function reads. |
| `context.py` | Automatic CONTEXT block per request: review-integrity stamp (request_id/timestamp/head_sha/base_sha/report_sha256) + previous decision/task, roadmap, `in_flight` counts (tasks in progress, how many hold an unpublished candidate — the numbers `contract.NEXT_WORK_PREFERENCE` reads), git summary, changed files, validation summary.
