Labels ride on the payload (`stats["tiles"]`, each row carrying its state, its `GROUPS` key and its label) so the template spells no state itself; the quarantine takes the Roadmap group's own "needs a human" and `blocked` only ever WIDENS to "blocked on a dependency". `by_state` is gone with the roll-up it existed to compensate for: two dicts of the same numbers under different names is how the page came to have two words for one state. **The in-progress breakdown is the part that carries information:** `in_progress_rows()` splits it into published to its side branch (B10 should have completed it, so a non-zero count means something is not retiring) / holding a candidate that is NOT published / no candidate at all (a timeout, a refusal, an abandoned round), plus `unknown`. Twelve tasks held unpublished candidates on 2026-08-06, each pinning a `task_base_sha` and so each a `task_base_behind_head` park waiting to happen — the failure that stopped the loop twice on 2026-08-04, and a flat "23 in progress" says none of it. The candidate comes from the execution record and publication is decided against the remote the record NAMES (`intended_remote`, defaulted to the polled `origin` — most records carry none, and reading absence as "some other remote" would make the whole breakdown read unknown against live data); a branch at a different sha is not-published with BOTH shas in the detail; **an unreachable remote is `unknown`, never not-published**, while "no candidate" is decided from the record alone and so survives a dead network. **Convergence and the open-work splits:** completed against still-open, with retired tasks excluded from the denominator on BOTH sides (counting a deliberately dropped task as outstanding understates progress, counting it as done overstates it) and the denominator printed beside the percentage so the exclusion is visible; then the open work by priority band and by area (`_area_of` = the id prefix — inbox, auto, rt, port, dash), because 31 open reads differently once eleven are p2 and once eight turn out to be one over-split family. `branch_for()` was extracted from `merge_states` and is now shared, so the merge panel and this one cannot name different branches for the same task. Read-only and lock-free like the rest: it reuses the ONE cached `ls-remote` (`ref_shas`) and the one `_executions()` read, adds no network call, and does arithmetic on values already in hand. An empty `groups` renders `readable: false` and says the graph could not be read — a summary is the one panel where a fabricated zero would be believed. **Full descriptions, expandable (2026-08-18, dash-10):** the roadmap panel sent `id`, `title` and `priority` and nothing else, so the one question an operator has about a queued task — what does it actually say — could only be answered by opening `.autoloop/tasks.json` by hand, and that is the file the whole roadmap is steered from. `_grouped` now carries `description` (WHOLE, never sliced — a cut description reads exactly like a task that really is that short), `chars`, `ordinal` and `waits_on` on every row, and each live group renders as a `<details>` docket: ordinal in the left rail, id, serif title, priority chip, "waits on" chip, char count, and the description in a `pre` with `white-space:pre-wrap` (they are hard-wrapped plain text with ALL-CAPS heads, so a reflow would destroy shape that carries meaning). **The ordinal is information, not decoration:** it comes from one `ordinals` map built from `_ready_order`, i.e. the ready set in `next_ready()`'s own `(priority, id)` order, so everything that cannot be picked — in progress, quarantined, dependency-blocked, retired, done — gets `None` and renders a dash; a number beside an unpickable row would be a claim about dispatch order the loop does not honour. `waits_on` comes from the extracted `_waiting_on`, which `_blocked_detail` also formats from (chip and sentence cannot disagree), and is populated for the BLOCKED group only — `state_of` notes that a retirement usually still declares the dependencies it was planned with, so filling it in everywhere would hang a "waits on" chip on a task that waits on nobody. **Descriptions are untrusted text going into HTML** and pass through the page's `esc()` at every interpolation; the tests execute those helpers under node rather than grepping for `esc(`, via the `PURE_ROADMAP_START`/`END` markers around the pure row region. A static search box filters on ids, titles AND description text (half of what an operator looks for is only in the description) and re-renders through the same `renderRoadmap` a poll uses; open rows are restored from `RMOPEN`, because a successful priority save clears `LASTJSON` to force a rebuild and would otherwise snap shut the row being read. The panel's colours are `--rm-*` tokens declared on bare `:root`, under the guarded dark media query and under `:root[data-theme="dark"]`, with the priority BAND as its own ramp and never a status role — a p0 is urgent, not broken. `/api/priority` and its handler are untouched; the input moved into the opened body and nothing about the write path changed. Cost, stated rather than discovered: descriptions are the largest thing in `tasks.json`, so `/api/state` and the page's re-render signature both grow with the roadmap on a 2s poll. |
| `dashboard.py` | **2026-08-20 (roadmap-01), has a completed task's work ever landed?** `shipped_report` / `shipped_states` / `mentions_task_id` / `commit_subjects` / `resolve_commit` answer ONE question per COMPLETED task — is there a commit whose SUBJECT names the task id, and is that commit an ancestor of the base head — and answer nothing else: no source is parsed, no capability is judged, and nothing is written (no registry, no execution record, no merge, no ref). It exists because on 2026-08-17 four completed tasks held every merge sweep with no record naming the work they shipped, and all four were resolved by hand with exactly this query (audit-0001 at 07b659b, dash-02 at dd28dfa, pkt-02 at 95b77a1, pkt-03 as four "pkt-03, part N" commits at 0fcc1c6). Four states, and the last two are deliberately different things: `shipped`, `not-in-base`, `unknown` (the search ran and no subject names the id — NOT "not shipped", since the work may have landed under a subject that never named it), and `unverified` (the search failed, or git could not resolve a matching commit). `is_ancestor` is reused unchanged, so ancestry is still `merge-base --is-ancestor` and nothing else, and every match is retained with its own `in-base`/`not-in-base`/`unverified` classification — one indeterminate commit never erases a definite ancestor found on another. Matching is whole-token against the id alphabet from `tasks._ID_RE`, so `pkt-03` does not match `pkt-030`, `x-pkt-03` or `pkt-03.5`. The search is `git log --all` rather than a walk of the base head, because a commit that is NOT an ancestor cannot be found by walking the head it is not an ancestor of; a commit on no ref at all is the stated blind spot and reads as `unknown`. Read-only through `_run_status` (`--no-optional-locks`) like the rest of the module, and deliberately NOT in `collect()`'s payload — it walks every commit on every ref, and that panel polls every 2s. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §3f-quinquies. |
| `dashboard.py` | **2026-08-27 (witness-01), a completed task is judged by ANCESTRY rather than by whether a commit subject names it.** Measured 2026-08-25: `shipped-report` returned seven `completed_unwitnessed` rows and two of them (dash-02 archived, scope-02 live) had shipped — their subjects simply never named them — and the report could not say which five were real. `registry_disagreements` now takes an injected `record(task_id)` and consults it before classifying an `unknown` row: an ancestral candidate leaves the disagreement list (into the new `witnessed` list, so nothing vanishes silently), a record naming a sha the base lacks is the DEFINITE `completed_not_in_base`, a record that exists and cannot be judged joins `unverified`, and `completed_unwitnessed` survives only when nothing — live or archived — names a candidate. The reader is `merge_sweep.execution_record_ancestry`, imported LAZILY by the new `_record_witness` (that module reaches the git gateway, the policy engine and the execution store; this page's import graph must not) and wrapped so a raising reader is `unverified` rather than a 500. `shipped_report` / `disagreement_report` take `executions_dir`: `collect` hands over the state directory it already resolved once (port-06), and an unset one resolves through `_state_dir_or_note`, the loop's own rule — with `None` a real value meaning "there is none", reported in the row's own detail instead of silently reading as "nothing names a candidate". Subject matching becomes corroboration; the `not-in-base` and `unverified` branches are untouched. Ancestry proves the branch is ACCOUNTED FOR, not that its content is present. |
| `cli.py` | **2026-08-20 (roadmap-01):** `shipped-report` is the operator-facing half of the above — read-only, lock-free, safe beside a live loop, and it never acts on its own output (nothing is retired, completed, reopened, merged or re-prioritised by it). `--repo` selects the checkout whose commits are searched and `--base` the head every match is judged against (default `HEAD`); a base that will not resolve reports NOTHING rather than judging rows against a head nobody could read. `_format_shipped` prints each matching commit with its own verdict — pkt-03's four part-commits are four lines of evidence under one row — and prints the "NO MENTION is not evidence" caveat rather than implying it. Exit 0 means every completed task got an answer, and "no commit subject names this id" IS one; exit 1 means at least one row could not be judged, because "I could not look" must not read the same as "I looked". |
| `validation_env.py` | **Added 2026-07-31** (`docs/SECURITY.md` S27, `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4g). The validation-environment boundary: dedicated TEST database credentials reach the post-writer validation subprocess and nothing else. `VALIDATION_ENV_ALLOWLIST` is exactly `DB_HOST`/`DB_PORT`/`DB_NAME`/`DB_USER`/`DB_PASSWORD`/`SECRET_KEY` (the brief said `JWT_SECRET_KEY`; this repo reads `SECRET_KEY` — `core/security.py:11` — verified by a clean-clone collection, and no alias is accepted). `validate_validation_env_path` refuses a relative path, a missing/non-regular file, a symlink (before `resolve()`, so a link into the real `.env` is caught as a link), a file not owned by the running user, any group/world permission bit, and any location inside the checkout / state dir / `workers_root` / either publisher path. `parse_validation_env` refuses unknown keys (so it can never become a channel for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), duplicates, malformed lines incl. an `export ` prefix, empty values, missing keys, and secrets under 8 chars (closing redaction-by-length at load time instead). `repo_declared_db_name` reads `.env.example`'s `DB_NAME` — the one exact production marker this repo defines, refused as a `DB_NAME`; deliberately NO host refusal (`localhost` is where a legitimate test DB lives) and no name heuristics. `ValidationEnv` is not a dataclass on purpose (a generated `__repr__` would print the values into the first assertion diff); `.apply()` builds the validation subprocess env, `.redact()` scrubs summaries, `.describe()` yields names only. `strip_validation_vars()` is the removal side, used by `ClaudeCliRunner.run` (BOTH tool sets) and `worker_env()`. Loaded once in `cli._build_orchestrator`, handed only to `ImplementExecutor` and `Orchestrator._run_post_commit_validation`; `doctor` reports the identical checks. **Production marker configurable (2026-08-16, port-02):** `repo_declared_db_name(repo_root, example_file, db_key)` and `load_validation_env(..., env_example_file, env_example_db_key)` take where to look, defaulted to this repo's `.env.example` / `DB_NAME` (`DEFAULT_ENV_EXAMPLE_FILE` / `DEFAULT_ENV_EXAMPLE_DB_KEY`, pinned equal to `config`'s copies by `test_config_repo_section.py` — no back-import, since `config` imports `tasks`). WHERE to look, never WHAT to refuse: the forbidden name is still read out of the repository, so a config cannot forbid a name the repo does not declare. `cli._load_validation_env` AND `doctor.run_doctor` both pass `config.repo.*` — defaulting either would let doctor come back clean on a config a real run refuses. An empty `env_example_file` returns `""`, i.e. no refusal, the same as an absent file — and that opt-out is the EXACT empty string: `config._load_repo_section` refuses a whitespace-only or padded value rather than passing it through, since this function reads any blank marker as "no declaration" and would silently switch the refusal off. The six-name `VALIDATION_ENV_ALLOWLIST` is untouched and stays deliberately non-configurable. |
| `worker_env.py` | Worker-side isolation (Autoloop M2), **wired into production 2026-07-30**: `worker_env()` builds the scrubbed subprocess environment (`GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` — NOT `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, which does not suppress Apple's compiled-in system gitconfig — plus `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, SSH/askpass vars removed). `WorkerRepoManager` creates one isolated, no-remote repo per task (`git init` + a one-time local-path `git fetch`, never `git worktree add`, which would share the main checkout's `.git`) — `cli._build_orchestrator` always constructs one at `config.workers_root`/`config.worker_hooks_dir` (2026-07-31: NOT `config.workers_dir`, the old nested-inside-the-checkout default; see `validate_workers_root` below) and it takes precedence over `worktree.py`'s `WorktreeManager` in `_dispatch_task_postcommit`. `verify_worker_isolation(git, expected_hooks_dir)` reports remote/pushurl/insteadOf/followTags/mirror/credential-helper/hook violations — `git` must be built with `env=worker_env()` (`WorkerRepo.gateway(policy)`) or it reflects the CALLING process's ambient config instead; `doctor` runs this against a throwaway probe repo. **`validate_workers_root(workers_root, repo_root, state_dir) -> list[str]`, added 2026-07-31** (Autoloop worker-isolation hardening, `docs/SECURITY.md` S23/S25): refuses a `workers_root` that is unset, relative, or nested beneath the checkout, its `.git` (including — verified against a linked worktree, where `.git` is a POINTER FILE — the real gitdir it resolves to), the state dir, or either publisher path; called from both `cli._build_orchestrator` (refuses execution) and `doctor.run_doctor` (a `fail` check). **`WorkerRepoManager.quarantine(task_id, label) -> Path`, added 2026-07-31:** MOVES (never deletes) a dirty worker repo to a sibling `quarantine/<task_id>-<label>` directory when `Orchestrator._prepare_write_capable_worker` finds residue from a failed prior attempt, so a later round never silently commits content that failed its own validation — since wrk-01 (2026-08-18) NOT applied to a dispatch that passed the resumed-worker reuse gate, whose residue is the interrupted round's own resumable work (`reused_recorded_worker=True` skips only this check; `_verify_committed`'s residual-dirty refusal still guards the candidate). **`worker_repo_is_reusable(path, branch) -> bool`, added 2026-08-18 (wrk-01):** the read-only probe behind resumed-dispatch worker REUSE — True only when `path` exists, is itself the top level of a git repository, and has exactly `branch` checked out; `_dispatch_task_postcommit` asks it BEFORE stale-base reconciliation (`_rebase_execution_if_stale`, whose nothing-reviewed-yet re-base is skipped when it passes, so a recorded base merely behind head never quarantines-and-rebuilds a reusable worker and the record keeps its stale base) and reuses the recorded worker as-is when it passes — carrying the decision into `_prepare_write_capable_worker` so uncommitted residue there (the interrupted round's own partial work) is neither quarantined nor recreated away — and otherwise falls back to the unchanged `create()` path (missing dir recreated at the recorded base/branch; non-git or wrong-branch path hits `create()`'s "already exists" refusal — no repair, no deletion, record never rewritten). See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4c/§4e and step 1b of the §4b sequence. |
| `escape_detector.py` | **Added 2026-07-31** (Autoloop worker-isolation hardening, `docs/SECURITY.md` S24, OPEN — detection, not prevention). `enumerate_checkout_paths(git)` — every tracked (regardless of working-tree state), untracked-non-ignored, and ignored path, via `git ls-files` (never `git status`, which omits an unchanged tracked file and collapses an ignored directory to one entry). `snapshot_checkout(repo_root, paths) -> CheckoutSnapshot` — content sha256 / symlink target / executable bit per path, read off the filesystem directly (never through git's object store, which would reflect the INDEX for tracked files, not necessarily the working tree). `diff_snapshots(before, after, exempt=None) -> list[str]` — creation/deletion/content/symlink-target/executable-bit violations, paths only, never contents. **`exempt(path, prior, current)` (added 2026-08-16, dash-04)** is a caller-supplied predicate, so this module still knows no special paths of its own and a caller that passes nothing gets exactly the pre-existing behaviour; the only production caller is `orchestrator._operator_priority_exemption`, for an operator's immediate `tasks.json` priority edit, and the predicate it supplies must prove BOTH an unbroken chain of COMPLETED mutation records — in a ledger outside the checkout, for that exact task file, since the watermark captured before the agent started, terminating at exactly the observed after-state — AND that the bytes differ in nothing but `priority` (`tasks.MutationLedger.completed_chain` / `priority_only_change`). An agent editing that file still parks the loop, including one that reproduces a state an earlier legitimate edit passed through or merely announced — see `docs/SECURITY.md` S32. **`is_derived_bytecode(rel, *sides) -> bool` (added 2026-08-16, esc-01)** is the ONE class that diff reports nothing for: a `.pyc` directly inside a `__pycache__/` directory, carrying a tag an interpreter actually emits (`cpython-<digits>[t]`, or this runtime's own `sys.implementation.cache_tag`), whose sibling `.py` is present as a REGULAR FILE in every snapshot side the cache entry exists on (`sides` are those sides, not a union of key sets) — derived from a source that stays fully in scope and hashed, after three false loop-fatal parks caused by out-of-band imports (a dashboard restart, a `health --json` poll) recompiling caches mid-round. Still violations: a `.pyc` outside `__pycache__` (legacy layout, importable with no source), a `.pyo` anywhere (no supported CPython emits one — PEP 488 made it `.opt-N.pyc` — so it is authored, not compiled), a `.pyc` wearing a tag no interpreter emits (`mod.attacker.pyc` — the family shape is load-bearing rather than this runtime's tag, since the incidents' writer was a different process than the loop), an orphan cache entry, a cache entry whose sibling `.py` is a symlink (watched as a target string, never hashed) or vanishes mid-window, and any symlink/directory at a cache path; `.so`/`.pyd` are never exempt. Reaches `diff_worker_tree` too, since that delegates here. `find_symlink_traversal(repo_root, paths) -> list[str]` — for `Task.approved_paths`: refuses a path whose ancestor component (or the leaf itself) already exists on disk as a symlink, since a plain relative-looking string can still write through to outside the repository. Wired into `orchestrator.py`'s `_execute_with_escape_detection` (brackets the write-capable `TaskExecutor.execute()` call, non-audit only) and `_prepare_write_capable_worker`. Does NOT inspect the checkout's own `.git/` internals — scoped to the three `git status` categories over the working tree. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4e. |
| `publisher.py` | Autoloop M2, **wired into production 2026-07-30**: the only path through which a candidate commit is published. `provision_publisher_repo` idempotently creates a dedicated BARE repo (`state_dir/publisher.git`) with its own empty controlled hooks dir; the remote url is a **provision-time snapshot** (`publisher_url.json`, §4d) — the FIRST call copies it from the main checkout and persists it, every later call re-asserts the PERSISTED value (never a fresh read), so an operator changing `origin` is never picked up silently. `Publisher.import_candidate` fetches a candidate by literal 40-hex sha from a worker's local repo path and verifies it via `read_commit` (never a separate `cat-file -t`, which the policy whitelist would deny); `Publisher.publish` reuses `GitGateway.push_exact` unmodified, passed `expected_url=<snapshot>`. `reprovision_publisher(state_dir, source_git, remote, confirm=True)` is the ONLY function that updates the snapshot — no default makes it callable by accident, and nothing directive-reachable calls it; `python -m autoloop reprovision-publisher --confirm` is its sole caller. `redact_url` strips embedded userinfo before any url reaches `status`/`doctor`/a parked question. `Orchestrator`'s `publisher`/`publisher_url_snapshot` constructor params route `_dispatch_task_push` through it — `cli._build_orchestrator` always constructs and passes both. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4c/§4d. |
| `state.py`, `transcript.py` | Atomic crash-safe JSON state (schema **v3**: adds `task_execution`, the serialised `TaskExecution` for whichever task is running the produce-then-review commit path — a mismatched version refuses to load rather than migrating), stamped requests; append-only JSONL audit log. |
| `state.py`, `transcript.py` | **2026-07-31, deliberately NOT a schema bump:** `PendingRequest` gains `conversation_url`/`conversation_epoch`/`resends_used`/`last_send_outcome` and `LoopState` gains `conversation_epoch`/`rotations`/`last_rotation` — all defaulted, so an on-disk v3 session keeps loading. The one field that could be misread is handled explicitly rather than by default: an unbound request is adopted onto the loop's URL only while `rotations == 0` (before any rotation the global URL *is* every request's URL) and raises afterwards instead of guessing. |
| `state.py`, `transcript.py` | **2026-08-14, also not a schema bump (pkt-01):** `Phase.DELIVERING`, `LoopState.outbox_diff` (the patch inlined in `outbox`, carried so `_step_ready` can plan a chunked delivery without re-reading git — checked against the payload before use, so a stale one is dropped rather than acted on) and `PendingRequest.delivery` (a `ChunkedDelivery`: the ordered parts, a per-part cursor persisted after each confirmed readback, and the omission payload to fall back to). Both fields default to `None`, which is exactly right for a session whose payload was sent as one message; a phase value is additive and `Phase(...)` still rejects anything unknown. |
| `state.py`, `transcript.py` | **2026-08-16, also not a schema bump (auto-04):** `LoopState.stop_kind` + `stop_blocker_id`, because `stopped` now means two things — the reviewer's own `stop` (`"contract"`) and a fault the loop ended itself on (`"fault"`, `orchestrator._to_fault_stop`). Unlike the additions above, the default (`""`) is deliberately NEITHER real value: a pre-existing session recorded no classification, so every reader gates on the POSITIVE value it wants (`cli._cmd_smoke_browser` PASSes only on `"contract"`; `cli._is_fault_stop` halts continuous mode only on `"fault"`), which puts the ambiguous case on the harmless side of each check. |
| `transcript.py` | **2026-08-20 (prof-01), additive and not a schema bump:** the transcript now answers "how long did that take?" without a second metrics store. `Stopwatch` measures ONE operation on a monotonic clock and `stamp()` folds the result into the `data` dict of the event that operation already emits, under the single key `DURATION_KEY = "duration_seconds"` — no new event types (68 is already a lot), no new file. `stop()` is TOTAL: a clock that raises, a non-finite reading and a BACKWARDS reading all yield `None`, which `stamp` writes as an ABSENT key rather than a wrong number, and the first `stop()` latches the outcome so a re-stamped payload never grows. The same module holds the pure READ side used by `cli._cmd_profile`: `read_records` (tolerant per LINE, because the file is being appended to while it is read and the last line can be a partial write), `STAGES` (prepare / submit / review_wait / execute, each naming its measured event AND its historical `request_id`-paired event pair), `profile_stages`, `summarize` (count/median/mean/nearest-rank p90/total), `build_profile` (the read reduced to a payload-free `TranscriptProfile`) and `render_profile`, which takes that aggregate and never a record. |
| `transcript.py` | **2026-08-20 (prof-01), the two sources are never mixed.** `MEASURED` is the operation's own elapsed time; `GAP_DERIVED` is the difference between two `ts` values, which is the INTERVAL — it contains every fault, retry and wait inside the window, at the one-second resolution `state.utcnow_iso` writes. They are summarized as INDEPENDENT sample sets and rendered as separate rows, and independent is meant literally: a record that carries a measured duration still contributes its timestamp window to the same stage's gap set, because for `submit` and `execute` the gap is a strictly WIDER window that contains the measured operation rather than a competing estimate of it. So `measured.count` reads as "rounds that recorded their own elapsed time" and `gap.count` as "rounds whose window could be paired", the difference between the two rows is what the window swallowed, and the one arithmetic that would be wrong — adding them — is printed as a warning instead of prevented by dropping samples. `review_wait` has no measured form at all, by nature rather than by omission — the stage IS the wait — and says so instead of showing a fabricated number. |
| `orchestrator.py` | **2026-08-20 (prof-01):** three measured durations and one missing identifier. `_timing_clock` (class attribute, `time.monotonic`) + `_stopwatch()` are the whole timing path; `_step_ready` measures packet CONSTRUCTION onto `request_prepared`, `_step_submitting` measures the SEND at its call site — `client.submit(...)`, frozen the instant the transport returns, deliberately excluding both the attach, the controlled reload and the duplicate check ahead of it and the verdict persistence and reconciliation branches after it, all of which stay inside the `request_prepared` → `request_submitted` gap where the profiler already says the window is wider than the work — onto `request_submitted`, and `_dispatch_task_postcommit` measures the executor call onto `executed`. That last stopwatch starts BEFORE the `worker_repos`/`is_audit` branch, not inside an arm: production always takes `_execute_with_escape_detection`, so a stopwatch in the `else` would measure only what tests reach (see `docs/COMMON_ERRORS.md`). `executed` also now carries `request_id`, read from `state.last_response` at the emit site and omitted when there is none — before this it carried none, so `directive` → `executed` matched nothing and the loop's most expensive stage had no timing of any kind. Nothing branches on a duration and no early return stamps one, so a stage that did not complete records nothing rather than a partial number. |
| `cli.py` | **2026-08-20 (prof-01):** `profile` joins the read-only, lock-free command tuple beside `status`/`tasks`/`next-task`/`doctor`. `_cmd_profile` opens exactly one file (`config.transcript_file`, or the `--transcript FILE` override for an archived transcript — a rotated file or a copy taken off another machine), prints per-stage source/count/median/mean/p90/total, and touches no state, no registry and no repository — safe while the loop runs, which is when the question comes up. It prints AGGREGATES only: the transcript holds whole review packets and whole reviewer replies, so the read is reduced by `build_profile` first and `render_profile` receives floats and static stage labels — never a record, not merely never printing one (`docs/SECURITY.md` S36). |
| `executor.py` | `TaskExecutor` seam (`execute(directive, task)` → `ExecutionOutcome`); `NullExecutor` reports honestly today. `ExecutionOutcome` carries five produce-then-review fields (`task_branch`/`task_base_sha`/`candidate_sha`/`changed_paths`/`post_commit_validation`), all defaulted so every prior caller is unaffected. |
| `implement_executor.py` | **Added 2026-07-31** — the write-capable `implement`/`revise` counterpart to `audit/`: `ImplementExecutor` runs ONE `Edit`/`Write`-capable `claude -p` subagent (`implement_agent_runner`, built on `audit.agents.ClaudeCliRunner` — its tool set is now a constructor param, defaulting to the audit's read-only pair) against the task's own isolated worker repo, derives `changed_paths` from the worker repo's real `git status --porcelain -z -uall` (never the agent's claim — `GitGateway.dirty_entries_all`/`dirty_paths_all`, new), then re-runs validation. No `.autoloop/` writes, no Markdown report. `cli._build_executor`'s new `_DispatchingExecutor` routes `implement`/`revise`-of-a-real-task here and `audit`/`revise("audit")` to `AuditExecutor` — `orchestrator.py` is unchanged. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §7b. **Bounded by SILENCE since 2026-08-14 (`stall-01`):** `implement_agent_runner` takes a `policy=` and builds a `stall.WorkerTreeProbe` over the task's worker repo, so the write-capable agent is supervised rather than run under a wall-clock timeout — see `stall.py` below. A failed run of ANY kind now also reports what it left behind (`_partial_work` reads the numbers from the worker repo's own git state), and `changed_paths` is populated on the error outcome too; `orchestrator._dispatch_task_postcommit` returns on `status != "ok"` long before the commit path, so nothing there can commit partial work. **Ambiguity is resolved, not escalated (auto-04, 2026-08-16):** `ask_user` is retired, so `_agent_prompt` now carries `_SMALLEST_REVERSIBLE_READING` — take the narrowest, most easily undone interpretation, and write one `ASSUMPTION:` line per choice. `_extract_assumptions` collects those lines from the agent's own output VERBATIM — nothing dropped or shortened, since this feeds a durable record whose whole job is surviving the round that replaces `report_details`; bounding happens at render time in `packet.py` — anchored at line start AND refusing a `>`/`-`/`*` prefix, so neither prose *about* the convention nor an agent quoting the instruction it was given (`> ASSUMPTION: <what you assumed...>`) can be harvested as a disclosure nobody made, and rides them out on the SUCCESS outcome only — a failed round is never committed, so its assumptions would describe code the reviewer never sees. Unlike `changed_paths` this IS the agent's word, safely: it is only ever shown to a reviewer, never computed with. **The approved plan reaches the agent (plan-01, 2026-08-18):** when the task carries a `decomposition`, `_agent_prompt` appends it under `_DECOMPOSITION_HEADER` — labelled as agreed with the reviewer BEFORE any code was written, to be worked in order and not widened, with "say so in your report" as the route for a plan that turns out wrong. A task with none gets no heading rather than an empty one. Still one dispatch: nothing here runs per step. |
| `stall.py` | **Added 2026-08-14 (`stall-01`).** Progress-based stall detection, replacing `audit.agent_timeout_seconds` — an elapsed bound that never once caught a hung agent and destroyed six agents' in-progress work over 2026-08-05/06 (up to 631 insertions at a time; the losses are listed in the module docstring). Elapsed time cannot tell a large task from a wedged one. `supervise(handle, probe, policy)` bounds the LACK of progress instead: while the worker repository keeps changing the agent runs, and it is killed only after `stall_seconds` with NO filesystem change (default 1800), or at the absolute backstop `ceiling_seconds` (default 14400), which should effectively never fire and reports itself as a finding when it does. `WorkerTreeProbe` is the observation — `git status --porcelain -z -uall` through the policy-validated `GitGateway` plus per-path `st_mtime_ns`/`st_size`, never anything the agent reports about itself and deliberately not a raw filesystem walk (the probe's own `git status` refreshes `.git/index`, so a walk including `.git` would see churn every tick and the detector would never fire; the per-path stat is what catches repeated Edits to ONE file, which leave the path set identical). Every ambiguous case errs toward letting the agent finish: a probe that cannot observe never stall-kills, and a missing sample is "no evidence of silence", not silence. `StallReport.describe()` is the reviewer-facing account — silence, elapsed, files changed, lines written, and whether validation had started, because a stall with substantial partial work and one with none call for opposite responses. `supervise` is pure over an injected handle/clock/sleep; the real path is `spawn_supervised` (Popen with `start_new_session=True`, output to temp FILES — an undrained pipe would be a hang manufactured by the hang detector, and a temp file outside the repo cannot register as progress) plus `ProcessGroupHandle`, which signals the whole group so orphaned children cannot keep writing after the kill. |
| `implement_executor.py` | **2026-08-24 (exec-01), what an UNCOMMITTED round reports.** A round that fails validation, times out or whose agent errors makes no commit and no packet, so the reviewer answers from `ExecutionOutcome.summary` alone — and that summary named only the CAUSE (the failing command, the agent's error). Measured cost: brw-11 wrote 2,347 insertions across four attempts, committed none, and drew four identical `revise` directives, while an operator reading the worker diff called the task too big in one look. `_partial_work_note(changed, partial, with_counts=…)` is the one renderer the no-candidate branches now share: file count, line count, and — the part counts cannot supply — WHICH files, bounded by `PARTIAL_WORK_MAX_PATHS`/`PARTIAL_WORK_MAX_PATH_CHARS` with the number it dropped always stated, because a silent truncation reads exactly like complete coverage. The paths are the discriminator: 2,347 insertions is equally consistent with nearly-done and too-big, three files matching the plan and twenty-two across four subsystems are not. EVIDENCE, never the agent's account of itself — every field comes from `git status`/`git diff HEAD` in the worker repo via `stall.WorkerTreeProbe`, the text SAYS so, and `result.raw_text` (which the same packet renders separately) is not an input; the test that demonstrates it makes the agent claim 900 lines across 40 files it never wrote. Attached on the failed-validation, missing-`validation_cwd` and agent-failed branches; the kill branch gets PATHS only, since `stall.StallReport.describe` already states counts and two separately-measured numbers for one fact is worse than none. `_measure_partial_work` is taken BEFORE the authoritative validation run, in the same window as the `changed_paths` read — a run that leaves a `.ruff_cache/` would otherwise be counted as the agent's work. Deliberately NOT on the two branches that already answer: `not changed` means the status read succeeded and returned nothing, and the `GitError` branch could not read the repo at all. `measured=False` renders UNKNOWN and never an empty list, since "we could not look" must not read as "the agent wrote nothing". The success path is byte-for-byte unchanged — it is also the branch whose summary becomes the commit message. Scope note: `ExecutionOutcome` gained no field and `prompts.py`'s `implementation_review` template gained no slot; `{summary}` already reaches the reviewer, and `executor.py` was outside this task's approved paths. |
| `autoloop/tests/test_partial_work_report.py` | **Added 2026-08-24 (exec-01).** The three halves of the claim above, against real git repositories and whole `execute()` rounds: an uncommitted round states file count, line count and which files; every figure comes from git rather than the agent (`test_the_numbers_contradict_the_agents_own_claim` makes the stand-in agent lie and checks the round reports the one file it really wrote); and a round that commits is unchanged. Plus the fail-open cases as unit tests of the renderer — unreadable repo reports UNKNOWN and never zero, paths survive when only the counts failed, the bounded list names what it left out — and two confinement tests: validation's own `.ruff_cache` residue is not counted as the agent's work, and the main checkout is untouched. Fixtures and fake agents are the `test_implement_executor` ones; no `claude` CLI and no real validation binary runs. |
| `implement_executor.py`, `tasks.py` | **2026-08-25 (del-01), deleting a file INSIDE `approved_paths`.** The case scope-04/scope-05 deliberately do not reach, and the one where the prohibition bought nothing: `Edit`/`Write` over an authorized path could always reduce that file to nothing, so what was missing was removing the ENTRY — destroying a file cleanly instead of committing a zero-byte one (roadmap-01, 2026-08-18), and three specs were bent around the gap in one night (brw-14, port-05, shrink-01). `DELETE-FILE: <path>` is a THIRD request anchor with the same discipline as the other two (start of line, `-`/`*`/`>` refused), and its authority is `Task.approved_paths` — NOT the out-of-scope record the other two select from. Two authorities for two questions, never merged. New `tasks.deletable_paths` is the gate and it CALLS `tasks.unauthorized_paths` over `effective_approved_paths` rather than restating the rule, so an out-of-scope deletion is refused by exactly the code that records an out-of-scope write and the two cannot drift; an unscoped task gets `()` and can delete nothing. `TRACKER_PATHS` entries are refused although writing them is allowed — that grant exists so every task can APPEND a change note to a shared append-only ledger — unconditionally, including when the task's own scope covers the tracker, and reported as their OWN category, because "outside your approved paths" would be false for a path the task may write. `_apply_scoped_deletes` runs in the same window as the other two passes (before the `git status` read, so a delete-only round has something to commit; before validation, so the suite grades the tree that ships) and leaves any path in `TaskExecution.out_of_scope_paths` to them, so no path is touched by two authorities in one round. The unlink is the SAME `_remove_recorded_file`, now shared by all three gates: no absolute path, no `..`, no parent outside the worker repo, only a regular file or symlink — which is also what makes an echoed instruction inert, since no file sits at `<repository-relative path>`. No tool is added: `WRITE_ALLOWED_TOOLS` is byte-identical and `Bash` stays disallowed. `_scoped_delete_note` NAMES every removed file in the round summary, computed from what was unlinked and never from `result.raw_text`. See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §4e's 2026-08-25 amendment and `docs/SECURITY.md` S25. |
| `autoloop/tests/test_scoped_delete.py` | **Added 2026-08-25 (del-01).** The claim above end to end, against real git and a real `ImplementExecutor` driven through the orchestrator (the agent is stubbed and its double can only WRITE files, never delete one — the constraint the feature lifts, so the double must not have a shortcut around it either). Round 1 COMMITS the file round 2 deletes, so the deletion is of a TRACKED path and `commit_and_capture`'s `git add -- <path>` really has to stage a removal; an untracked-then-deleted path never reaches `changed_paths` at all and has its own test. Plus: the deletion in the reviewed range as a removal, a delete-only round reaching review, validation grading the post-deletion tree, a move as write-plus-delete, disclosure (the summary names the file while the agent's text says nothing about it, and an agent CLAIMING forty deletions moves no entry), and what stays refused — out of scope, every tracker, an echo of the prompt's own placeholder, a bulleted line, a directory, a `..` traversal out of the worker repo, and a path the out-of-scope record already governs. |
| `prompts.py`, `config.py`, `cli.py` | Strict `PromptTemplate` library (+ `audit_kickoff`, `smoke_test`, `postcommit_review`); strict TOML config (`[executor]`, `[audit]` sections; new path properties `workers_dir`/`worker_hooks_dir`/`executions_dir`/`intents_dir`/`seed_tasks_file`/`continuous_fingerprint_file`); `run [--continuous] / status / tasks / next-task / doctor / smoke-browser / pause / resume / unlock / reset / reprovision-publisher` CLI with locking on mutating commands. `_build_orchestrator` always constructs the full produce-then-review collaborator set (see the intro above). `run --continuous` loops the phase machine, resuming a saved non-terminal phase and otherwise auto-selecting a ready task or running one audit per repository fingerprint change (`cli.repo_fingerprint`), sleeping — with ZERO Claude/ChatGPT calls — when neither applies. **`_reconcile_retired_blockers`, added 2026-08-16 (task-retire-01):** closes the open `blockers.Blocker` records of every RETIRED task via `archive_stale` (a machine reason, never a forged `answer`), because the blocker list is read independently of the registry by `start`, `health.check` and the heartbeat — so retiring a quarantined task used to leave the loop stopped on a question about superseded work. Called from `_cmd_retire_task`, from `_cmd_start`'s preflight and at the top of every `_run_continuous` iteration (out-of-process readers, so waiting for exhaustion would leave `health` alarming while the loop works). **Scoped by KIND as an allowlist** — only `task_fatal` quarantines, whose question really is about the one retired task. Every `loop_fatal` record survives regardless of the task id it names, because a loop-wide park (`checkout_escape_detected`, `primary_checkout_dirty`, a worker/publisher environment failure) routinely names whatever was in flight, and closing it on a retirement would manufacture resolution of the safety condition and let `start` proceed; an unrecognised/empty `kind` reads as loop_fatal, matching the fail-closed default in `_to_needs_user`. `(loop)` blockers are never swept either. **Operator-question wording (auto-05, 2026-08-16):** the `clarification` template and `run --answer` (help text + phase refusal) describe the question they carry as the LOOP's own — `state.question`, from a `_to_needs_user` park — not a reviewer's, since the decision that raised one is retired and cannot reach a park (`docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §9c). Wording only; preconditions, payload fields and dispatch untouched. |
| `tests/` | 805 hermetic tests — no network, no playwright, no live claude CLI (see `docs/TESTS.md`). The figure is the previously documented 793 plus the 12 the loop-02 token hardening added (hand-counted, not a re-audit of the total — the aggregate has trailed the collected count before). `test_self_upgrade.py` additionally guarantees, with an autouse fixture, that no test can replace the pytest process via `os.execv`, and with a second one that no test leaves an `AUTOLOOP_EXEC_HANDOFF_TOKEN` behind for the next. |
| `prompts.py`, `config.py`, `cli.py` | Strict `PromptTemplate` library (+ `audit_kickoff`, `smoke_test`, `postcommit_review`); strict TOML config (`[executor]`, `[audit]` sections; path properties `workers_dir`/`worker_hooks_dir`/`executions_dir`/`intents_dir`/`blockers_dir`/`seed_tasks_file`/`continuous_fingerprint_file`, plus the **required, absolute** `[paths].workers_root`, 2026-07-31 — see below); `run [--continuous] / status / tasks / next-task / blockers / answer / doctor / smoke-browser / pause / resume / unlock / reset / reprovision-publisher` CLI with locking on mutating commands (`blockers` is read-only/no-lock; `answer` locks). `_build_orchestrator` always constructs the full produce-then-review collaborator set, including a `BlockerStore` (see the intro above), and now REFUSES (`ConfigError`) before doing so if `worker_env.validate_workers_root` reports `config.workers_root` unsafe. `run --continuous` loops the phase machine, resuming a saved non-terminal phase and otherwise auto-selecting a ready task or running one audit per repository fingerprint change (`cli.repo_fingerprint`), sleeping — with ZERO Claude/ChatGPT calls — when neither applies. **Blockers (2026-07-31):** a `task_fatal` `needs_user` park (`_handle_parked_task`) quarantines just that task and clears the session so the loop continues with other READY tasks; a `loop_fatal` park still stops the loop. Exhaustion (no ready task, unchanged fingerprint, >=1 open blocker) prints every open blocker and exits 0 instead of sleeping forever. **`_RESOLUTION_PRECONDITIONS` (worker-isolation hardening, 2026-07-31; +3 same-day follow-up keys):** `answer` re-checks an environmental blocker's condition rather than trusting the operator's text — `git_failure_budget_exhausted` (renamed from the dead `git_failure_budget`), `worker_environment_drift` (now a dedicated `verify_worker_isolation`-based recheck, not the browser/login probes it used to share with `login_expired`), `worker_isolation_violation` (reuses the same recheck), `push_refused_protected` (now actually emitted — see `orchestrator.py` below), `primary_checkout_dirty` (dedicated `_precondition_checkout_clean`, re-runs `GitGateway.is_dirty()`), `checkout_escape_detected` (its OWN unconditional-refusal precondition, `_precondition_checkout_escape_detected` — NOT `is_dirty()`-based: the escape detector's snapshot covers untracked+ignored paths `is_dirty()` cannot see, so a checkout-cleanliness recheck would clear on an escape that touched only an ignored path, e.g. `.autoloop/state.json` itself — found in a second round of review and fixed same-day), `publisher_url_drift`, `push_refused`, `login_expired`, `submission_ambiguous`. Verified exhaustive against every code `orchestrator.py` actually emits by an AST walk (catches a stale key), plus a curated reverse list (catches a missing one — added same-day; the AST walk alone cannot) — both in `test_m1_hardening.py`. **`[audit]` timeout retirement (2026-08-14, `stall-01`):** `agent_timeout_seconds` split into `agent_stall_seconds` (write-capable silence window, 1800), `agent_ceiling_seconds` (absolute backstop for both paths, 14400) and `audit_agent_timeout_seconds` (the old elapsed meaning, kept ONLY for read-only audit agents, which change no files and so have no progress to observe). A config still naming the retired key LOADS and is handled explicitly rather than ignored: `_migrate_retired_timeout` consumes it (so it never reaches `AuditConfig`), migrates its value onto `audit_agent_timeout_seconds` — the one replacement that keeps its exact meaning — and returns an operator-facing message in `AutoloopConfig.migration_notices`; an explicit `audit_agent_timeout_seconds` takes precedence, and a junk value is refused like any live setting. `load_config` stays PURE (no stream, no "already warned" global), so the notice text is order-independent; `cli.emit_migration_notices` — called from the thin `cli.load_config` wrapper every command reads its config through — prints it to **stderr** (never stdout, which `status`/`tasks`/`next-task` pipe) at most once per process, since `run --continuous` reloads config every round. A stall window at or above the ceiling is refused too: it would read as configured while being unreachable. **Fault stops (auto-04, 2026-08-16):** `_is_fault_stop` / `_report_fault_stop` — `stopped` is now two terminals, so `run --continuous` halts (exit 2, printing the recorded blocker route) on `stop_kind="fault"` instead of treating it as the clean boundary that would kick a fresh session into the same wall, plain `run` exits 2 on one rather than reporting a completed round, and `smoke-browser` PASSes only on `stop_kind == "contract"` — without that gate the first denied reply (the smoke policy allows zero denials) would end in `stopped` and be announced as a healthy round-trip. All three read the POSITIVE value, so a legacy `""` is a clean boundary and an unclassified future fault is a failure. **New `[repo]` section (2026-08-16, port-02):** `RepoConfig` — `env_example_file`, `env_example_db_key`, `audit_report_glob` — what the loop cannot infer about the TARGET repository, each previously a constant naming THIS repo. Every default is the exact constant it replaced, so a config omitting the section behaves identically to the code before it existed (pinned by `test_config_repo_section.py`'s first test, which is the premise of the whole change). **NOTHING here grants authority** — each setting says WHERE to read something the repository itself states, and the one repo-specific constant that *does* decide what an agent may write (the always-approved `tasks.TRACKER_PATHS`) is deliberately NOT a key: a `tracker_paths` setting was written and withdrawn in review, since this file is gitignored and a suffix blocklist cannot make an implicit grant documentation-only (`docs/SECURITY.md` S31). A config still naming it loads and is handled explicitly by `_migrate_retired_tracker_paths` — consumed before `_check_keys`, value DROPPED, operator notice appended to `AutoloopConfig.migration_notices` alongside the retired-timeout one. Strict like every other section otherwise: `_check_keys` refuses an unknown key, `_repo_relative` refuses an absolute/`..`/padded path for both path settings and glob metacharacters for the non-glob one, a non-table `repo = "x"` draws a `ConfigError` naming the section rather than `dict()`'s raw conversion error, the disable is the EXACT empty string for both path settings (a whitespace-only value is refused, not stripped — `repo_declared_db_name` reads a blank marker as "no declaration" and would turn the application-database refusal off while reading as configured), and `env_example_db_key` must be a plain variable name — a key with a space or an `=` could never match a parsed line, so it would silently read as "no marker declared" rather than refusing. **`_clear_fault_budget_on_answer` (budget-01, 2026-08-17):** answering a `fault_attempt_ceiling` blocker resets `TaskExecution.fault_attempt_count` to 0 and nothing else — without it the answer unblocks the task and the next dispatch parks on the identical wall, which is the mechanical hand-repair this task removed. Narrow on purpose: only that code (answering `attempt_count_ceiling` refills NOTHING, since that budget bounds the task's own failing work), only that counter (`candidate_sha`, `review_round`, `last_revise_feedback` and every `attempt_ledger` entry are preserved, so the history of what was spent survives the fresh allowance), and best-effort (an unreadable record must not turn a successful `answer` into a failure). **Running the code it just merged (loop-02, 2026-08-18):** `_self_upgrade_at_boundary` is the ONE place the loop replaces its own process, reached only when `Orchestrator.run` returns `SELF_UPGRADE` inside `_run_continuous`. It preflights the merged tree in a SUBPROCESS first (`_preflight_import` — `python -c "import autoloop, autoloop.policy, …"` in the merged checkout, since this process already holds the old modules and a reload would produce exactly the half-swapped state `execv` avoids), refuses when the merged checkout is not the tree this process imported from, flips the record to `execed` BEFORE the call so a merge that imports and then fails at runtime cannot loop, arms the lock's `exec_handoff` (which mints a one-use token into this process's environment — the exec is what carries it to the successor, so nothing may spawn a child between the arming and the call, and the preflight therefore runs before it), and only then `os.execv(sys.executable, [sys.executable, "-m", "autoloop", *sys.argv[1:]])` — the documented launch shape rebuilt, because `argv[0]` under `-m` is `__main__.py` and re-running that as a script breaks its relative imports. Every carry-on path SETTLES the record (`_settle_upgrade`, clearing it if it cannot be written), because a record left `pending` makes every subsequent round hand the process back — including the path where `os.execv` itself raises, which settles `exec_failed` after disarming the handoff (review round 2, 2026-08-18): `execed` means "a successor is running", so leaving it there would have the next iteration's confirmation report a replacement that never happened. `_confirm_self_upgrade` retires the one-shot after one full `_run_continuous` iteration under the new code — checked at the TOP of the second iteration, since every branch below ends with its own `continue`. Plain `run` reports the pending upgrade and exits 0 without replacing anything: its argv carries flags that are not safe to re-run. New config path property `pending_upgrade_file` (`.autoloop/pending_upgrade.json`). See `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` §3f-quater. |
| `tests/` | 810 hermetic tests — no network, no playwright, no live claude CLI (see `docs/TESTS.md`). |

## Config + entry

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `Procfile` | `web: uvicorn backend.main:app` |
| `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` | Dependency CI (S15). `pip-audit` (backend) + `npm audit` (frontend) on push/PR to main + weekly cron. |
| `.github/workflows/tests.yml` | Test CI (rt-10). Three jobs on push/PR to main: `lint` (`ruff check .`), `pipeline` (`pytest tests/`) and `docs` (an inline stdlib check of the four change-note trackers and `docs/audit_charters.toml`). The harness job that ran `pytest autoloop/tests` (+ the `isolated` marker run) was removed by port-05, 2026-08-26. The backend suite is **not** gated — blocked by hb-01; see the CI section of `docs/TESTS.md`. |
| `.env.example` | Env schema. Real `.env` is secrets — never read it. |
| `.gitignore` | |
| `.gitattributes` | **New 2026-08-19 (docs-01), and deliberately RULE-FREE.** It declares no merge attribute at all; it exists to record why `merge=union` on the two change-note trackers was tried, measured and removed the same day. Git cannot scope a merge attribute to a REGION, and union never reports a conflict, so the attribute meant "these files can no longer conflict" rather than "combine the appended notes" — it swallowed a genuine prose conflict — and it resolves per LINE, so it duplicated a grown 19,410-character row instead of merging the two additions. Combining notes moved to `autoloop/note_merge.py` + `auto_merge.AutoMerger._merge`. Was pinned by `autoloop/tests/test_docs_merge.py` (exact emptiness, plus both union failures demonstrated against real git) — that suite is no longer collected by anything this repository runs (port-05, 2026-08-26), so nothing here enforces the emptiness today; docs/TODO.md **#45** tracks re-homing it. |
| `requirements.txt` | Delegates to backend's requirements. |
| `pytest.ini` | Root pytest config. `testpaths = tests` since port-05 (2026-08-26) — a bare `pytest` covers the pipeline tree alone; it named `tests autoloop/tests` from 2026-08-04 until then. `addopts = -p no:randomly -m "not isolated"` is UNCHANGED and both halves are load-bearing: the plugin block works around a spaCy/thinc seed conflict, and the marker keeps a flaky-in-company test out of every shared run (nothing under `tests/` carries it today, so that half is inert here and kept deliberately). There is deliberately no `-n` here: parallelism belongs on the individual command so it cannot silently reach a run whose point is one process per test. There is no root `conftest.py`. |
| `README.md` | Repo-root readme. |
| `CLAUDE.md` | This project's master guide (read first). |
| `docs/TODO.md` | Bugs + tasks, ordered by blocking dependency. |
| `docs/AUTOLOOP_TODO.md` | Open work on the loop itself — the live-progress gap during agent fan-out, the unexplained changeset-publish refusal, execution records stranded by a `workers_root` move, and the self-hosting rollback design. App tasks stay in `docs/TODO.md`; security findings stay in `docs/SECURITY.md`, referenced by id. |
| `docs/AUTOLOOP_REMOVAL.md` | **Added 2026-08-26 (port-05).** The operator manifest for taking `autoloop/` out of this checkout: what to `git rm`, what to keep and why, and the one import (`scripts/seed_validation_db.py:48`, docs/TODO.md #46) that must be lifted first. This repository's document, not the harness's — it is deliberately absent from its own removal list, so it survives the operation and becomes the record of it. |
| `docs/WORKFLOW_AUDIT.md` | Full word-learning trace with numbered holes. |
| `services/book_import_service.py` | **Document-package import orchestration (roadmap A2).** `verify → validate → dry-run → persist`, no DB mutation before every gate passes. `list_packages`, `validate_package`, `import_package`, `dry_run_package`. Scope is narrow by design: no reconstruction (A4), segmentation (A5), AI review (A8) or worker invocation (A10). |
| `services/document_package/` | The A2 internals. `contract.py` (versions, element enum, coordinate space, 1-based page-numbering base — no logic), `issues.py` (fatal/warning/info collector), `loader.py` (discovery, checksums, **path-containment chokepoint**), `validators.py` (individually testable structural validators), `coordinates.py` (the single Docling↔fitz flip), `persistence.py` (`PersistenceBackend` seam + dry-run + the A3 placeholder), `result.py` (`import_result.json`). Imports nothing from `nlp_histo`/Docling/Torch — asserted by a test. |
| `evaluation/` | **Document-ingestion evaluation harness (roadmap A1).** No LLM, no `nlp_histo` import, no Docling/torch. `normalize.py` (the single shared normalizer every offset depends on), `annotation.py` (gold format + round-trip), `metrics.py` (Stage 1 fidelity + Stage 2 boundary/reader), `model.py` (`PredictedDocument` + the `DocumentSource` adapter seam), `adapters.py`, `segmentation.py` (spaCy `de` baseline), `runner.py`, `report.py`, `compare.py`, `baselines.py` (frozen constants), `cli.py`. Run: `python -m evaluation run --label X`. |
| `benchmark/documents/` | Gold corpus for the harness — original German text, three files per document (`.blocks.json` input, `.gold.txt` expected units, `.gold.json` sidecar). See `benchmark/README.md`. The real graded readers are commercial and stay in gitignored `files/`. |
| `docs/INGESTION_PIPELINE.md` | Design for the document-ingestion boundary: offline `nlp-histo` worker → versioned document package → import → reconstruction → `book_sentences` → sentence-by-sentence reader. Package schemas, coordinate conventions, AI-review op contract, evaluation plan, task breakdown. ROADMAP **N7** / TODO **#44**. |
| `docs/TESTS.md` | Test inventory, coverage gaps, known failures. |
| `docs/COMMON_ERRORS.md` | Symptom-first log of errors actually hit here (tooling, tests, build, lint, research). Grep it by the error text before debugging. |
| `docs/SUMMARY.md` | This file. |
| `docs/audit_charters.toml` | The per-domain briefs this repository's read-only audit agents are given — one `[[domain]]` table each, in wave order, carrying `slug`/`title`/`charter`/`model`. Read read-only, once per run, from the root of the checkout being audited (`[repo].audit_charters_file`); shipped here since port-03 so the knowledge travels with the code it describes. `DEFAULT_DOMAINS` in `autoloop/audit/executor.py` holds the same six domains as the fallback for a repository that ships no file, and `autoloop/tests/test_audit_charters.py` requires the two to stay equal. A file that exists but does not parse — or is a directory, or cannot be read — aborts the audit rather than falling back. |

---

## How to find things — common questions

| Question | Open |
|---|---|
| Where is the state machine? | `lexy-app/backend/services/progression_service.py` (`_RULES`) |
| Where does a transcript click go? | `routers/words.py:record_transcript_click` → `usage_events_service.record_transcript_click_event` (atomic dedup via unique partial index); only on a new insert does it then call `progression_service.apply_progression("transcript_clicked")`. |
| Where do SRS cards come from? | `progression_service._update_srs` — created on `passive_srs/active_srs="create"` or first correct event |
| Where is the LLM called? | `services/llm_service.py` — all calls go through here, cached via `llm_cache_service` |
| Where is the polymorphic `(item_id, item_type)` join? | `review_service.get_due_cards` is the canonical example; `usage_events_service` analytics too |
| Where do book pages render? | `frontend/src/components/BookReaderPage.tsx`, blocks via `book_service.get_page_detail` |
| Where do free-chat words match against vocab? | `chat_service.match_learning_words` (words via direct SQL; phrases via `matcher_service.match_sentence_with_ids`). |
| Where are reading selections wired into main progression? | `routers/reading.py:156` calls `find_catalog_item` then `apply_progression("status_marked_learning")` |
| Where is `users.settings` JSONB read/written? | `services/settings_service.py` — defaults live here |
| Where do notifications get written? | `subtitle-scraper/pipeline.py:_notify_user` for success (`video_done`, `channel_done`); `_mark_request` emits `request_failed` whenever `status='failed'`. |
| Where is auth enforced? | `core/deps.py:get_current_user` — every router uses it as a Depends |
| What seeds run on startup? | `main.py` lifespan: `phrase_service.seed_from_blueprint_map`, `grammar_service.seed_rules`, resume pending content_requests |
| Where is the ChatGPT engineering loop? | `autoloop/orchestrator.py` (state machine); full doc in `docs/AUTOLOOP.md` |

---

## Change notes — append ONE new line at the END of this file

Where a task records what it changed when there is no natural new row for it
above. **This section stays last in the file, and a note is appended at the
very end of it.**

Five rules. They are not style — they are the precondition the loop's own merge
path checks before it will combine two branches' notes
(`auto_merge.AutoMerger._merge` → `autoloop/note_merge.py`; the shape is pinned
by `autoloop/tests/test_docs_merge.py`, the reasoning is in `CLAUDE.md` §12):

1. **Add a line. Never grow a line.** Do not append your note into an existing
   table row or paragraph. The resolver only combines whole lines added AFTER
   everything that was already here; a grown line is an edit, and the merge
   stops. That habit is how one row above reached 19,410 characters.
2. **One note, one line.** Keep it to roughly a sentence (~400 characters). If
   it needs more, add a second line, or put the detail in the section above
   that owns the file and leave a pointer here.
3. **Never edit, delete or reorder a line someone else wrote.** The resolver
   refuses the whole merge if you do, and that refusal is the point: a
   rewritten claim needs a human to look at it.
4. **Order is arrival order, not chronology.** A merge concatenates one
   branch's appended lines then the other's. Read the `Date` and `Task`
   columns, never the position.
5. **Call the marker `CHANGE-NOTES`; never write the comment out again.** The
   section below opens with an HTML comment the resolver finds by that text,
   and it requires the marker to appear EXACTLY ONCE in this file. A second
   copy — the easy way to quote it while documenting how any of this works —
   makes the resolver decline every parallel merge, silently. That is the
   original failure with an extra step, and it is how docs-01 itself first
   shipped.

Adding a genuinely new table row anywhere above is fine and always was — that
is also "a new line". This section exists for the notes that have no row.

Nothing may follow the last note line: this section is the end of the file, so
that the next task's append lands at the end of the ledger rather than inside
whatever came after it.

<!-- CHANGE-NOTES: append below, one line per note, at the END of the file. Never edit a line above. -->

| Date | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|

Naming one path under both forms is not an error: the removal pass runs FIRST and the revert is reported superseded, so restoring base bytes can never quietly undo the deletion the same report asked for. |

No TTL, no head key, no per-branch verdict, nothing kept between sweeps. | 2026-08-24 | dash-21 | Deliberate consequence, stated rather than hidden: a sweep that raises now fails every caller joined to it, where before each connection failed alone. The alternative — a joiner running its own sweep when the leader fails — is the concurrency this exists to deny. The waiter release is in `finally` and the `except` is `BaseException`, so a `KeyboardInterrupt` or `SystemExit` in the leader releases everyone; the page treats the result as a failed poll and retries on its next tick. |
